It is very difficult to identify the person who has the greatest credit for the idea of creating KSAP. The second reason, which is extremely important, is the very role and responsibilities of this institution. Let me remind you that KSAP had and is to educate apolitical government officials. Art. 153 of the Constitution states that civil service acts to ensure the professional, fair, impartial and - as should be stressed - politically neutral performance of the State's tasks. However, the President is a politician, but he was a politician. [[Oh, Jesus!]] Finally, the last argument, the physical nature. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u68	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on its work on the government proposal for a law amending the law on the functioning of hard coal mining and certain other laws, Seym printing Nos 722 and 753. The project was addressed to the Committee on Energy and the Treasury for consideration at the 23rd Sejm meeting on 19 July 2016. The Commission worked on it at its meetings on 21 July 2016 and 5 September 2016 and the result of the committee's work is a report in Parliament's printing No 753. The draft amendment concerns, in view of the very important economic and social objectives of the State, the need to amend the laws: the Law of 7 September 2007 on the functioning of coal mining and the Law of 30 August 2013 on the grant for certain entities. It is also the result of the creation of a division: the economy with fossil deposits and the creation of a budget section 48: the economy with fossil deposits. Revision of the provisions cited above. the laws are necessary in view of the performance of the tasks enshrined in those laws by the energy resort. It is therefore necessary to remove the discrepancies preventing the mobilisation of funds from the special-purpose reserve for the financing of tasks carried out in the course of the decommissioning of mines, cover services in the form of mining leave and leave for employees of the coal processing plant and to cover the current production losses of enterprises. It is also necessary to amend the provisions relating to the financing of budgetary subsidies for the tasks carried out in the energy department, inter alia. This is the extension of the funding period for these tasks until 2020 for the Salt Mine Bochnia sp. z o.o. due to the limited financial resources provided for in the budget laws in relation to the amounts included in the approved decommissioning programme and in addition to the programme for the decommissioning of additional bottom-up works. The amendment also justifies the need to allow financing from 2017 onwards from the budget subsidy of the operations carried out after completion of decommissioning work carried out by the Mine of Sulfur Machów in liquidation. The draft also transferred specific powers to companies operating in the electricity, oil and gas sectors from the Minister responsible for State Treasury to the Minister responsible for Energy. At the second meeting of the committee, on 5 September this year, two important parliamentary amendments and legislative and editorial amendments tabled by the Parliamentary Legislative Office were presented to the draft submitted in print No 722 and were adopted by the committee after the discussion. The project is not subject to public consultation as it allows for the continuation of restructuring processes in hard coal mining on terms already agreed with the social side. However, the adoption of the project entails financial implications for the state budget. This is the sum of 101 100 000. PLN in the years 2016 and 2020 resulting from the amendment of the provisions of the Law of 30 August 2013 on grants for certain entities. It is also about the amount of 61 125 thousand. zł for the liquidation programme for the Salt Mine Bochnia sp. z o.o. The budget will also be charged an amount of 4872 000. PLN per year, total of 19 488 thousand. zł, because this amount is planned to be spent on financing operations after completion of decommissioning works in Piaseczno mining plant carried out by Miań Sierka Machów in liquidation. From the amendment adopted at the committee meeting on 5 September this year. results in an increase in the amount related to the budgetary expenditure limit referred to in Article. Article 8a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 11f Act of 7 September 2007 on the functioning of hard coal mining, in the years 2015 to 2018 up to a maximum of PLN 7 billion. The project, 722 and 753, is compatible with European Union law and does not contain technical regulations and therefore does not require a notification procedure in its case. Following the examination of the amendments tabled at the meeting on 5 September 2016 and after their positive opinion, the Commission for Energy and the Treasury asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u70	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend the adoption of a law amending the law on the operation of hard coal mining and certain other laws. The proposed law is intended to amend the Act of 7 September 2007 on the Functioning of Coal Mining, the Law of 18 March 2010 on the specific powers of the Minister of State Treasury and their implementation in certain capital companies or groups active in the electricity, oil and gas sectors and in the Law of 30 August 2013 on grants for certain entities. As a result of the amendment of the Government Administration Act of 19 November 2015 and the amendment of the Law on the Functioning of Coal Mining of 22 December 2015, and following the entry into force of the Finance Minister's Regulation of 2 December 2015 concerning the classification of budgetary parts and the identification of their contributors, the budgetary resources intended to finance the processes related to the restructuring of the hard coal mining sector were included in the budget section 48: The Kosalin Economics. Since the authority empowered to conclude grant agreements for the financing of the tasks laid down in the Act on the Functioning of Coal Mining remains the Minister responsible for energy matters, there was a discrepancy preventing the mobilisation of funds from the special reserve for the financing of tasks carried out during the decommissioning of mines, as well as cover services in the form of mining holidays and leave for employees of the coal processing plant and to cover the current production losses of the company. The proposed amendments aim at removing the gap. With regard to the amendments to the Law of 30 August 2013 on a grant for certain entities, the proposed provisions aim to allow financing from a budget grant at the disposal of the Minister of Energy of operations carried out after completion of decommissioning works in the Piaseczno mining plant carried out by the Mine of Machów SA in liquidation and to extend the grant period for tasks carried out by the Salt Mine Bochnia until the end of 2020. Mine of Sulfur Machów S.A. in liquidation has so far received a grant under the Law of 14 July 2000 on financial restructuring of sulphur mining in accordance with the agreement concluded with the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. This agreement provided for the financing of the Liquefying and Recultivating Works of the Machów Sulphur Mine by the end of 2015. This deadline was postponed until the end of 2016 due to the need to complete the work. Failure to provide the means to carry out the task of maintaining the drainage system from the water reservoir set up in the Piaseczno excavation site may contribute to the uncontrolled reconstruction of the water mirror in the areas adjacent to the excavation site. In such a situation, both agricultural crops and residential buildings would remain in direct danger of submersibles. In order to counteract these adverse effects, a drainage and drainage system is needed. Therefore, the granting of a budget grant appears to be the only way to protect the areas adjacent to the Piaseczno excavation from damage. There is also a need to extend the subsidy period for tasks carried out by the Bochnia Salt Mine until 2020. This is particularly the case for work aimed at eliminating the historic part of the plant and taking action to ensure proper traffic safety and securing historic excavations. It should be borne in mind that the Bochnia Salt Mine is a unique monument, and some of the excavations due to the undoubted historical advantages have been included in the UNESCO World Cultural and Natural Heritage List. Action is needed to restore extremely valuable historical excavations and to eliminate areas that threaten them. The proposed amendments to the Law of 18 March 2010 on the special powers of the Minister responsible for State Treasury matters and their implementation in certain capital companies operating in electricity, oil and gas fuels sectors are intended to avoid any disputes of competence between the Minister competent for State Treasury and the Minister competent for Energy or other entities exercising ownership rights in shares of State Treasury companies. [[♪ The bell ♪]] In the proposed amendment to the law on the operation of hard coal mining, the definition of a single-member company of the Treasury carrying out tasks in repairing the damage caused by the operation of the mining plant after its liquidation was added. The amendment of the law on the operation of hard coal mining is also intended to supplement the tasks of repairing mining damage caused by the operation of the mining plant in such a way that the damage caused by the operation of the mining plant during the liquidation and as a result of operations carried out after the liquidation is completed. Therefore, we consider it necessary to adopt this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We proceed with the examination of item 3 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending certain laws defining the conditions for conducting innovative activities (print No 789). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Science and Higher Education of Mr Piotr Dardziński to present the justification for the project. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u162	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour and pleasure to present a draft law on the amendment of certain laws defining the conditions for conducting innovative activities, sometimes called the small or first law on innovation, which is a proposal for changes which Polish entrepreneurs expect and which allows them to expand their innovative activities. The bill is the result of teamwork - board and team for: innovation. It is also unique in the sense that two ministries were appointed to present the position of the government, because the Ministry of Finance, represented by Minister Leszek Skiba, is also attached to the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. Two sentences, briefly, before I talk about the project itself. Two sentences on the strategy in which it was prepared. I mentioned that this project is referred to as the first draft of the Innovation Act. By the end of the year, we would like to prepare the second Innovation Act. Why did we divide this legislative process into two parts? This is a natural question that is probably born in your heads. The answer is: because we wanted to introduce these solutions as quickly as possible, which have a common consensus mainly in business environments, but also in the government itself, Prime Minister Beata Szydło. These are solutions that can make it easier for entrepreneurs to invest in R &amp; D in a clear and fast way. It is about cooperation between economic operators and Polish universities. That's why we wanted to make a quick change. At the same time, we had a broad consultation which would result in a White Paper to be presented by the end of this month, and by the end of the year we would like to prepare this second, larger law on innovation. What is the content of the project that I have the honour to present to the High House? Firstly, these are issues relating to tax solutions. It abolishes permanent taxation of income tax on the import of intellectual and industrial property, which is brought into companies by universities, but not only by universities. This solution exists in the current legal state, while it is limited temporarily to the years 2016 and 2017. We would like to show Polish entrepreneurs that the strategy to support innovative businesses, i.e. innovative enterprises, is a long-term strategy, not a short-term test, hence the abolition of this time limit. Secondly, the Act includes an increase in the amount of the deduction of costs for R &amp; D activities and an extension of the list of costs mainly by the expenditure associated with the patenting process or the registration and protection of intellectual property. Entrepreneurs will be able to reduce their tax burden if they invest in R &amp; D. At the same time, they will be able to qualify for these R &amp; D activities, including those related to the protection of intellectual property. We would like the activity of recording this property to be much greater on the side of entrepreneurs and scientists. These amounts, these write-down thresholds are raised, and I'm not going to go into details right now. It's going to be about twice as much as the current state. Thirdly, we are extending the scope for deduction of costs for R &amp; D. In the current legal situation there is a limit of 3 years in which these write-downs can be applied. Entrepreneurs and scientists have generally pointed out that, in the case of innovative projects, not only innovative but long-term, we are dealing very often with a situation in which there is no income in the short term, it is not possible to apply these write-downs, because the company is simply in a period where it is still waiting for potential profits. Therefore, we extend this period from 3 to 6 years. As an alternative to long waiting for a return, we offer companies a write-off, a cash refund. This cash refund will only be for new entrepreneurs, up to 2 years from the moment of registering business, i.e. for so-called. Start-ups. This is a solution that we would particularly like to dedicate to those who start their business. That is why I said that this is an alternative solution, because the trader himself will decide whether, in a situation where no write-off is possible due to the above mentioned lack of income or lack of income, he will be able to benefit from a cash refund of 18% or 19% - it depends on the system in which it is accounted for - whether it will wait for 6 years for possible income to apply a 100% write-down which is guaranteed in the first solution. Here I would like to draw your attention to the fact that these solutions proposed by us have their flexibility. They are addressed after consultation to different groups of entrepreneurs, they are a response to the needs that entrepreneurs have informed about. The second block of changes in this law are issues that concern changes in the field of science and higher education. In the public consultation, entrepreneurs often point out that they are looking for university partners who will be competent to speak the language of entrepreneurs about joint ventures. We wouldn't want to turn scientists into businessmen like we don't want to turn entrepreneurs into scientists. We would like people who are competent to understand both sides when it comes to this cooperation to come into contact with these two groups. That is why we want to dedicate some of the grants that are allocated to universities and research institutes to support research potential, to activities related to the commercialisation of research. This means that universities and research institutes will now be required to dedicate part of this grant to those persons or organisational units in their operation which are engaged in the commercialisation of research. This is a loud-sounding postulate that says that we need a professional staff that is capable of supporting scientists in cooperation with entrepreneurs. Secondly, the principles of management of research results for the defence and security of the state financed by the NCBR have been made more flexible. Currently, the results of such research are always taken over by the State Treasury. In this act, the Ministry of National Defence, for which we are very grateful, showed a much more flexible attitude and the situation will be such that only after the expression of interest by MON of the law will they pass to the Treasury. If the MON doesn't express any interest, the scientist will be able to commercialize these findings in the civil system. This information will be included in the contract to be signed by the trader by joining the grant implemented by the NCBR. The Ministry of National Defence will always have the right to reserve the possibility of a possible exclusive licence for these solutions. We are also making modifications, here is the issue of radical reduction of the various bureaucratic procedures associated with the so-called procedure. the patronage of scientists, i.e. the acquisition by them of property rights to inventions which were related to their responsibilities, whether at universities or at institutes. The situation at this point is that previous solutions have forced the university administration to remake all possible inventions that appear in their field of activity, not just those that have the potential to defy them. This in turn results in the extension of this process in the case of scientists who are waiting for the agreement of the university, because the documents are being processed, including those where there is no request for commercialization of research. Again, responding to the demands of universities and scientists, we reverse this procedure and we say: the university should deal with these proposals, where the scientist is interested in commercialisation in the first place, or actually exclusively. This is more or less a reversal of the proportion and a 95% reduction in the bureaucratic responsibilities of universities. Another element is to remove the time limit. Currently, we have a solution that works for up to 5 years when creators of inventions have shares in the benefits of commercialization. In a natural way... I have recently had the pleasure of taking part in a debate in the economic forum in Krynica, in which Mr KGHM's President was asked by the editor-in-chief of the Republic to say one recommendation to the Ministry, which should be made immediately to mobilise cooperation between science and business. The Chairman of the Skin said on second thought: "This must be worthwhile for scientists." That's the answer to the fact that it has to pay off to scientists. If we have limited commercialisation opportunities, benefit from commercialisation for up to 5 years, and as I said earlier, these benefits often appear much later, scientists are not motivated to approach these projects. It seems to us that this restriction is unjustified and that is why we are proposing that it be terminated. We are also increasing opportunities, and we are explaining in more detail a question of interpretation that exists today, which is related to how many special purpose vehicles a university or research institute may have. We clearly point out that it does not have to be one company, these special purpose companies may be more, which will also activate universities to cooperate with entrepreneurs. Other changes that no longer concern science and higher education, i.e. making the rules for public legal entities having infrastructure, including research, more flexible. Here in cases... You know, the laws relating to national property say that any possession of such property which exceeds a certain limit should be addressed to the Minister of the Treasury for permission to dispose of such property. We would like to allow the possibility for research institutes, PAS institutes or universities to take decisions without the approval of the Minister of the Treasury, unless these decisions concern issues which have consequences for more than 90 days. So if someone rents, rents up to 90 days, then they simply do not apply for permission, they can lease, share apparatus, research spaces, conference rooms, etc. etc. The last point is to enable financing of projects related to the implementation and operation of the environmental technology verification system from the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. This system allows to obtain internationally acceptable confirmation of the efficiency of the technological solution, which is related to the area of environmental protection. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The Act will enter into force 30 days after its publication, with the exception of tax provisions and those concerning the rules on the financing of science which will enter into force from 1 January 2017. Since this draft law contains, as I have mentioned, tax arrangements, we are obliged to maintain specific standards in the process of adopting them, which you know better than I, including the preservation of a long enough vacathio legis, in order to provide taxpayers with the opportunity to become familiar with the solutions they have adopted. Since these solutions should be known to entrepreneurs as soon as possible, and because they really expect these solutions, I will humbly and kindly ask the High House to proceed as quickly and as quickly as possible with this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u217	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Report from the Health Commission on the Government's draft law amending the Food Safety and Nutrition Act, print No 786. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, sent the above draft law to the Health Commission for first reading on 23 August 2016. The Health Commission, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 5 September, brings forward: The High Sejm will adopt a draft law from print no. 786 without amendments. The amendment of the Law of 25 August 2006 on food safety and nutrition, hereinafter referred to as the Act, results from the need to implement the provisions of Directive 2015/254 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 11 February 2015 repealing Council Directive 93/5/EEC on the assistance of the Commission and the cooperation of Member States in the scientific examination of matters relating to food, hereinafter referred to as Directive 2015/254, and the implementation of Commission Regulation No 579/2014 of 28 May 2014 derogating from certain provisions of Annex II to Regulation No 852/2004 of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards the transport of liquid oils and fats by sea, hereinafter referred to as Regulation 579/2014. In the case of Directive 2015/254, point 16 of the repealed Council Directive on the assistance of the Commission and the cooperation of the Member States in the scientific examination of food matters, hereinafter referred to as Directive 93/5/EEC, should be deleted from reference No 1 to the Act. The deadline for the implementation of Directive 2015/254 was 29 February 2016. The repealed Directive 93/5/EEC laid down requirements for Member States to take the necessary measures to ensure that their competent authorities and bodies cooperate with the Commission and to assist them in a scientific examination in the public interest of food matters, in particular public health. It also lays down that the provisions adopted by Member States shall contain a reference to this Directive or shall be accompanied by such reference on the occasion of their official publication. Member States were to determine how such reference was to be made. In the Republic of Poland, a solution was adopted concerning this directive in reference No 1 to the Act in the section concerning directives in point 16. The repealed Directive 93/5/EEC was of a technical and organisational nature and the issues of cooperation between the European Commission in the field of food safety with the Member States and the European Food Safety Authority. Food Safety lays down the provisions of Regulation No 178/2002 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 28 January 2002 laying down the general principles and requirements of food law, establishing the European Food Safety Authority. Food safety and procedures for food safety, hereinafter referred to as Regulation 178/2002, which apply explicitly. In accordance with Regulation 178/2002 the tasks of the Scientific Committee on the The foods referred to in Directive 93/5/EEC have been forwarded to the European Food Authority. Food safety and the rules for cooperation are laid down in Article. 90 laws. In order to harmonise the provisions of the Act with the provisions of Regulation 579/2014, a new wording should be added to the article. 70 of the Act, delete paragraph 22 in reference No 1 in the section on transposition of the Directives and add point 55 in reference No 1 in the section on implementation of the Regulations. Regulation 579/2014 shall apply directly from 18 June 2014. The provisions of Directive 96/3/EC have been implemented in the Regulation of the Minister for Health of 21 December 2010 on specific hygiene requirements for the transport of raw sugar, liquid oils and fats in bulk by sea. In view of the fact that the abovementioned Directive has been repealed and that the requirements for the transport of liquid oils and fats are explicitly laid down in Regulation 579/2014, it is appropriate to amend the wording of the authorisation provided for in Article. 70 of the Act by deleting part of the authorisation to the Minister responsible for Health with regard to the issue of the transport of liquid oils and fats by sea. It is planned that the draft law will enter into force 14 days after its publication. The proposed law is not subject to a notification procedure within the meaning of the provisions of the Council of Ministers Regulation of 23 December 2002 on the functioning of the national system of notification of standards and legal acts. The bill, together with a message to the public consultation, was published on the subject page of the Public Information Bulletin of the Ministry of Health in accordance with the provisions of the Act of 7 July 2005 on lobbying activities in the process of law-making and the Public Information Bulletin of the Government Legislative Centre. The draft law is compatible with European Union law in accordance with the position of Minister of Foreign Affairs of 5 May 2016. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u223	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, 15 I am pleased to present my position on the amendment of the Food Safety and Nutrition Act. The update of the existing provisions is dictated by the need to implement the provisions of Directive 2015/254 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 11 February 2015 repealing Council Directive 93/5/EEC on Commission assistance and cooperation between Member States in the scientific examination of food issues and the implementation of the provisions of the Commission Regulation of 28 May 2014 derogating from certain provisions of Annex II to the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards the transport of liquid oils and fats by sea. The proposed law adapts national law to these EU regulations. The amendment is an orderly, systematic regulation of existing legal solutions. The deadline for the implementation of Directive 2015/254 was 29 February 2016. The question therefore arises as to why this amendment is being examined only today, after more than six months. Shouldn't changes in Polish legislation dictated by changes in EU law be carried out in parallel so that Polish legislation keeps up with changes and is as up-to-date as possible? I hope that such simple amendments to the national law, which are mainly editorial, will be developed and processed without undue delay, as there are no serious reasons for delaying the amendment at the time of entry into force. With regard to Directive 2015/254, the draft law proposes that in reference No 1 to the Act, point 16, including the repealed Council Directive 93/5/EEC of 25 February 1993 on the assistance of the Commission and the cooperation of the Member States in the scientific examination of food matters, be deleted. In December 2012, the Council adopted Regulation The Commission has launched a regulatory efficiency and efficiency programme to provide a simple, clear, stable and predictable regulatory framework for businesses, workers and citizens. In this context, the Commission has undertaken to repeal provisions which have become redundant or obsolete. Council Directive 95/5/EEC was intended to ensure the smooth functioning of the Scientific Committee. Food by promoting scientific support from Member States and by organising cooperation with relevant national authorities on scientific issues relating to food safety. The repealed Directive lays down the requirements for Member States to take the measures necessary to ensure that they cooperate with the competent authorities and bodies with the Commission and to assist them in the scientific examination in the public interest of food matters, and in particular public health. As regards Regulation 579/2014, which has been in force since 18 June 2014, it is necessary to harmonise the provisions of the Food Safety and Nutrition Act with the provisions of the Union Regulation by giving a new wording to the article. 70 of the Act and deletion of point 22 in reference No 1 in the section on transposition of the Directives. In view of the above, Kukiz15 will vote in favour of the above law. Thank you.

This procedure is a logical consequence of the adoption by the European Parliament and the Council of Regulation No 178/2002 on 28 January 2002. They shall communicate the provisions of that Regulation to the European Parliament and to the Council. The tasks of the Scientific Committee for Food Safety are as follows: Food referred to in Directive 93/5/EEC as repealed. The second task to be implemented is to implement the provisions of Commission Regulation (EU) No 579/2014 of 28 May 2014. This Regulation concerns derogations from certain provisions of Annex II to Regulation No 852/2004 of the European Parliament and of the Council. It explicitly sets out requirements for the transport of liquid oils and fats by sea and repeals Council Directive 93/43/EEC on the hygiene of foodstuffs with regard to the transport of liquid oils and fats by sea. It is therefore necessary to delete from the Article. 70 of the Food Safety and Nutrition Act authorising the Minister responsible for. health as regards a matter governed by Commission Regulation (EC) No 579/2014. Given that the deadline for implementing Directive 2015/254 was 29 February 2016, the Free and Solidarni Circle will vote in favour of the Act. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Food safety (EFSA) and they are currently provided for in Regulation (EC) No 178/2002. In Poland, he was appointed to cooperate with the Agency in 2008. EFSA's Polish Coordination Point. Commission Regulation (EU) No 579/2014 of 28 May 2014 grants a derogation, the wording of which is "By way of derogation from point 4 of Chapter IV of Annex II to Regulation (EC) No 852/2004, liquid oils or fats which are intended for human consumption or are likely to be used for human consumption ('oils or fats') may be transported by sea vessels not intended for the transport of foodstuffs, provided that the conditions laid down in Article are met. 2 and 3 of this Regulation. According to the arguments set out in the Regulation, these amendments are due to the practicality and nuisance of food business operators to the requirement in point 4 of Chapter IV of Annex II to Regulation (EC) No 852/2004 of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards the transport by sea of liquid oils and fats which says that foodstuffs in bulk, in the form of liquid, granules or powder, must be transported in containers or containers, containers intended for the transport of foodstuffs. As the European Commission points out, access to sea-going vessels for the transport of foodstuffs is too limited to ensure the continuity of trade in such oils and fats. The conditions for the application of this derogation in the abovementioned Regulation No 579/2014 describe the prior cargo in containers prior to bulk transport by sea vessels of liquid oils and fats, both those to be processed and those not to be processed, as well as the material from which the vessel is made. The report of the Health Commission of 5 September 2016 on the draft amended act requests the adoption of the draft without amendment. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u235	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the law on the income of local government units. The need for the proposed amendments is linked to the loss, at the end of this year, of the existing provisions of the Act of 13 November 2003 on the income of local government units governing the rules governing the establishment of payments of voivodships to the state budget and the way in which they are distributed. It is therefore necessary to introduce this law. The absence of the proposed regulation will deprive the majority of voivodships of a significant part of the revenue, as there will be no legal basis for determining both payments and subsidies, and local authorities will not receive regional subsidies from payments in 2017 and subsequent years. It should be noted that, unlike voivodships, there is a correction and equalisation mechanism for municipalities and powiats without a time limit in the law on the income of local government units. The draft law on the amendment of the law on the income of local government units extends for 3 years, i.e. for the years 2017 and 2019, the rules of the correction and equalisation system for voivodships in force in 2016 apply. At that time, our government, the Government of Law and Justice, will prepare a comprehensive law that will regulate this issue. The project follows the principle that no voivodship will receive less resources than it would have received in 2017 and in 2018 than if the law had been in force before 14 November 2013. In line with the assumptions, as in 2016, a special reserve will be created in the state budget for 2017 and in 2018 and 2019 respectively, which will be used to fund the construction, reconstruction, renovation, maintenance, protection and management of voivodship roads. The draft law also contains changes aimed at ordering and clarifying the current rules, namely the provision of information on the amount of subsidies via electronic tools and the determination of the minimum amount of subsidy transferred between the entities covered by the administrative breakdown of the country. It is proposed that transfers between units should be subject only to amounts higher than the lowest remuneration. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law on amending the law on the income of local government units. Thank you very much.

It also includes mechanisms for building complementary grants for individual voivodship units, local governments of voivodships. The construction of this mechanism was designed here in such a way that the reserve supplementing the revenue of local governments, the grant for construction, renovation and maintenance of roads was constructed in such a amount - and this is apparent directly from this law - that no local government of voivodships would receive income lower than that which it would have received if the 2014 solutions were in force. Therefore, this mechanism is directly enshrined in this law and obliges the government to include such a reserve in each draft budget for each year, so that the amount of that reserve for the purposes I have mentioned in its entirety does not result in revenue being lower than that which would have been if the 2014 system had been in force. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u259	The draft law on the amendment of the law on the income of local government units extends for 3 years, i.e. for the years 2017 and 2019, the implementation of 2016 solutions in the field of correction and equalisation for voivodships. The project also envisages other changes of order and clarification of the existing rules, such as the transmission of information on the amount of subsidies via electronic tools, the determination of the minimum amount of the subsidy transferred between the entities covered by the administrative breakdown of the country. The amendment currently being proposed is necessary because the existing provisions of the Act of 13 November 2003 on the income of local government units governing the rules governing the establishment of payments of voivodships to the State budget and the way in which they are distributed are in force until 31 December 2016. The introduction of new solutions in the field of correction and equalisation for all local government units, municipalities, powiats and voivodships should be embedded in the target financial and economic system. This will make it possible to properly define the new principles and criteria of the corrective and equalisation system, taking into account the actual, after reforms, the income potential of local government units. This time will allow you to develop the most optimal solutions. Taking into account the time needed to introduce reforms and then to collect revenue allocation data between local government units, it should be agreed that 2019 will be the most optimal date for developing a new correction and compensatory system that has been in force since 2020. The absence of the proposed regulation would mean that local governments would not receive regional subsidies from contributions in 2017 and subsequent years, as there would be no legal basis for establishing both payments and subsidies. This would deprive most voivodships of a significant part of their income. Unlike voivodships, there is a correction and equalisation mechanism for municipalities and powiats in the Act on the Income of Local Government Units, without a time limit. The proposed law therefore regulates the rules for establishing contributions to the State budget and their distribution only in relation to voivodships. For these reasons, PSL supports the proposed solution.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u260	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 6 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the law on gambling and some other laws (print No 795). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Wiesław Janczyk to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u261	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Act on Gambling and some other laws contained in print No 795. The proposed bill constitutes a fundamental amendment to the Act on Gambling adopted in 2009. It includes systemic solutions that introduce particularly necessary changes in the regulation of gambling. The government has prepared this project with two main objectives: firstly, ensuring the highest level of protection of players against the negative effects of gambling and raising public awareness of the dangers of illegal gambling, secondly, the fight against the grey zone in the gambling environment, especially in the area of online gaming machines and gambling. High Izbo! Allow me to present the most important assumptions of the project, which, according to the government, will ensure that the objectives are achieved. The project provides for an extension of the gaming directory that can be offered legally on the Internet. At the same time, in order to ensure the safety of players, the organisation of these games outside mutual betting and promotional lotteries will be covered by the state monopoly. This will ensure that those seeking entertainment can do so safely in games organized by a trusted player, ensuring the highest level of player safety. A similar protection will be given to players playing slots. Those willing to play will be able to do so legally not only in casinos, as so far, but also in arcades run by a single-person company of the Treasury. As part of the protection of players common to games on the Internet and in slot arcades, we propose the use of mechanisms for responsible games. The operators of these games will be required to introduce the rules of the responsible game. They are to be defined, among others. procedures to verify the player's age, the obligation to include in the player's visible way information about the player's playing entity, the obligation to register players and mechanisms to enable players to control their activity and limit the amount of funds involved in the games. The purpose of the introduction of the above mentioned paragraph is to: mechanisms are in particular the protection of minors, as well as those prone to addiction. The solutions presented are the implementation of the European Commission Recommendation of 14 July 2014 on consumer protection rules and gambling players online and on the protection of minors against online gambling. Although the recommendation refers only to gambling On-line, we decided to apply the standards included there also to slot games. This is due to the priority given to protecting players in this project. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, At the beginning, special attention was paid to online gambling. The project gives state authorities the opportunity to order the blocking of the websites of illegal operators as well as the obstruction of payments to them. A register of domains will be created to offer gambling in breach of the law. The register will be kept by the Minister of Finance and will contain the names of online domains used to offer gambling to players in an illegal manner. Access to websites using domain names in the register will be blocked by telecom operators providing Internet access services. The entry in the register shall be of a material nature and shall be subject to opposition by the Minister of Finance within 7 days by means of an administrative decision. The decision shall be subject to an action directly before the Provincial Administrative Court in Warsaw, thus keeping the register and making entries will be subject to judicial administrative review. High Izbo! The second way to limit the gray area on the Internet will be to remove links from websites offering illegal gambling to make a quick deposit with the payment service provider, e.g. The bank. To this end, it will be prohibited for payment service providers to offer and make available their services on the pages included in the register. It should be noted that the changes resulting from the introduction of the register do not impose difficult obligations on traders. The register shall be kept in an electronic system enabling the automatic transmission of information to the ICT systems of telecommunications operators and payment service providers. The list of domain names entered in the register will be published on the Minister of Finance's website, kept publicly and generally accessible to all persons and entities concerned. The project also provides for a three-month transitional period for obliged entrepreneurs to comply with the new requirements. We are aware that many Poles have so far unwittingly used the services of illegal operators on the Internet. The changes in the project foresee that any person who tries to access the website via the domain in the register will be redirected to the Minister of Finance. There will be information about the illegal nature of the game in which it was intended to participate, a link to information about entities legally offering online gambling and information about the penalty for participating in illegal gambling. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, As part of the fight against the grey zone, the project also provides for an extension of the catalogue of entities subject to monetary penalty for breach of the rules governing the gambling market. These include operators of gambling in breach of a licence or permit granted, the owner of a utility premises where unregistered gaming machines are located and where business activities are carried out. The responsibility of persons acting as managers or members of the management bodies of gambling operators without the required licence, authorisation or notification has also been introduced. In addition, the penalty will be imposed on entities in breach of the proposed rules on the obligation to block websites and payments made to operators offering illegal gambling as well as to operators of national monopoly gambling and participants in illegal games. In order to combat the grey zone as effectively as possible, it will be prohibited to have slot machines by unauthorised operators. The ban is to be excluded, among others. manufacturers of vending machines and traders carrying out their transport, which will ensure the freedom to conduct business activities of Polish and EU entrepreneurs. The level of penalties for violating the rules governing gambling has been raised. For example, the penalty for organising games on slots without the required license has been increased from 12 thousand. PLN to 100,000. Evil for every vending machine. Thus, the preventive function of penalties has been strengthened. High Izbo! The project is expected to enter into force on 1 January 2017. It should be noted that the reason for this project was not the expected additional budgetary revenue, but the result of the reduction of the grey area. We estimate that the additional revenue in the first year of the Act's new wording will amount to PLN 729 million and will ultimately reach more than PLN 1.5 billion. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, At present, the draft law has been submitted to the European Commission in the framework of the notification process. Government representatives shall remain in constant contact with the Commission in order to carry out this process as efficiently as possible. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! By passing the bill to the High House for consideration, I assure you that from the government we are ready to participate in parliamentary work with full commitment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u273	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I will present my position on the draft law from print No 795. The project introduces numerous amendments to the Act on Gambling of 19 November 2009 and its main objective is to eliminate the so-called "gambling." a gray area for games on slots and games on the Internet. Hence, the project proposes a number of legal tools to combat illegal gambling. The fact is that the current level of State control over the gambling market is insufficient, which, with significant illegal activities particularly in relation to slots and online games, makes such activities not subject to tax and reduces the revenue of the state budget. In our view, the gambling market needs to be sorted out, and it is worth noting here that this project does not make any changes in taxes related to gambling. Neither the tax rates nor the form of their settlement are altered. On the one hand, the project significantly extends the legal offer of gambling and on the other hand introduces strict control from the state. The expansion of offers and their state monopoly consists of introducing numbers games and lottery money into the Internet, arranging games on slots not only in casinos, but also in slot arcades and offering online gambling. The project also assumes the liberalisation of the rules of poker and allows casino games to be organised via the Internet by a company implementing the state monopoly. Similarly, a state monopoly is introduced in slot arcades which are considered to be the most addictive and socially dangerous. The project is also addressing the issue of protecting players from the negative effects of gambling, as well as raising the level of public awareness about the risks. To this end, the possibility of blocking access to websites offering illegal gambling is introduced, banning payment services from being made available by providers of these services to illegal operators, thus obstructing payments. All applicants for a licence or authorisation to engage in gambling activities will be required to draw up and apply the rules of the responsible game to be approved by the Minister of Finance. Responsibilities related to the rules of the responsible game include the inclusion on the website of information, full information about the organizer of the game, his gaming permit, the ban on games for minors, the risks associated with gambling and the institutions providing assistance in the fight against gambling problems. In addition, it is envisaged to introduce the need to register the player before joining the games, allow participants to control their activity on the website and prevent participation in the game after the funds have been exhausted in the player's account. The draft provides for an extension of the catalogue of entities subject to monetary penalties for infringements. The penalties will no longer be imposed only on operators who organise gambling in breach of a licence or a permit, but also on operators who own at least the premises, those who violate the law of blocking websites, payment agents, and those who perform managerial functions in non-licensed gambling operators. Illegal gambling operators will be subject to more severe penalties, e.g. The penalty will be increased from 12,000 for playing the automaton without the required license. Up to 100,000. zł. This amount will be charged to each slot machine. The amount of the penalty will be based on estimates of average revenues received from games on slots. It is adjusted to the level of actual losses incurred by the State Treasury. This project is quite extensive. It also proposes concrete and detailed solutions in the form of draft regulations of the Minister of Finance. It was made available for consultation, many comments were submitted and included in the project. [[♪ The bell ♪]] To sum up, it is a project that is very, very much expanding the offer of gambling, exploits ICT achievements and at the same time introduces state control in this area. We are in favour of directing the project to further work on the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u304	Thank you very much, Minister. The discussion is closed. A request was made in the discussion to reject the draft law at first reading. We proceed with the examination of item 7 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on countering unfair exploitation of the advantage in trade in agricultural and food products (print No 790). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development of Mr Zbigniew Babalski to present the reasons for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u305	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the government a draft law against unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in trade in agricultural and food products. High Izbo! The food supply chain is a very important element of both national economies and global economies, and its importance is not only economic, but also - and perhaps above all - social dimension. For the proper functioning of the food supply chain, it is essential to maintain a good relationship between all its links. The structure of the food supply chain makes it particularly vulnerable to unfair commercial practices. These are common practices which are grossly different from good commercial behaviour and are contrary to the principle of good faith and fair trading and are unilaterally applied by one trading partner to the counterparty. It is very important to be aware that the use of unfair practices at the level of food distribution weakens the financial condition of food producers and agricultural raw materials, reduces the investment potential of food producers, reduces their innovative activities and thus reduces the quality of the goods produced. Consequently, these factors have a negative impact on the last link, which is the consumer. There is no doubt that adverse events occur in all European Union countries with varying intensity and different nature of their practices. The lack of response to these problems from the EU institutions, in addition to the creation of a platform for voluntary rules, the effects of which are very limited, has forced Member States to regulate their national legislation. The Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development also took such action by drawing up a draft law on countering unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in the marketing of agricultural and food products. The draft law sets out rules against practices consisting in unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage by one of the parties to the contract. It covers suppliers of agricultural or food products as well as buyers of these products, protecting them from the use by one of the parties of unfair use of the contract advantage. The draft law designates the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection as the competent authority for practices involving unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage. The President shall initiate proceedings in cases of unfair practices which use the contractual advantage of the contract. This gives protection against the applicant's identification of the notification, which is a flaw in the existing solutions. The Act sets out sanctions for unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage and for preventing the President from carrying out checks to that extent. Here I refer you to the article. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, Article 34 and Article 34 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 It describes how there are also proposals for the level of sanctions that the President of the Office may impose. The draft law also amends the Act on the Agricultural Market Agency, introducing penalties for the purchase of agricultural products without a written contract, i.e. Chapter 5, Article. 41 that regulates, there is talk of a 10-percent proposal when it comes to punishing an unfair supplier or a recipient. The tasks arising from the Act will be carried out by the President of the Office using the provisions of the Act of 16 February 2007 on the protection of competition and consumers. I inform the High House that the project was adopted by the Council of Ministers on 19 July. I also inform you that if the Sejm accepts the same project, it will enter into force six months after the date of the announcement. This time is needed to refine all kinds of implementing rules that will accompany these changes. I would like to conclude by saying that the issue of unfair treatment of operators is widespread. Therefore, without regulation, the Minister for Agriculture has taken such an initiative in order to defend primarily agricultural producers, food producers and consequently the consumer. I hope that, after the discussion, the High House will direct the project to further work in the committee, and there will be any doubts that will arise, we will try to explain, solve problems. Thank you very much, Marshal. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u306	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jan Krzysztof Ardanowski, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u307	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Law and Justice Club an opinion on the Government's draft law against unfair exploitation of contract advantages in the marketing of agricultural and food products from print No 790. Our club welcomes and will support the government bill. I would like to make it clear that farmers and food producers have waited years for such solutions. In fact, there are very many economic operators in the market, which, out of fraud, have made thieving a way of achieving business success. This bill, I think, will counter it. In fact, information is getting to us that, for example. trade networks are trying to transfer trade tax recently passed by Parliament to suppliers, forcing them to reduce their prices. This bill is also intended to counter this. First of all, the Act regulates turnover between large entities, does not regulate, which is probably obvious, the sale of small quantities, small turnover between entities, because it specifies that the minimum turnover which entitles the Act to act is at least 50 000. annually, it concerns large companies or groups of capital, which have turnovers exceeding PLN 100 million. As a general rule, the Act on the trading of a supplier with a cooperative or a group of producers of which he is a member does not apply, it is obvious. The Act gives the President of the UOKiK a tool to carry out inspections in order to obtain information that may constitute evidence in the case. At the same time, it introduces a very fast way of suing the District Court in Warsaw, it is a question of checking operations, specifying the possibility of bringing an action within 7 days of the act being carried out. The President of the UOKiK also has to refer the case to the court within 7 days, which also has only 7 days to investigate the complaint. This will eliminate all kinds of delaying deadlines. The President of the Office may give immediate enforcement of the decision if the application of the practice threatens the continued functioning of the trader for whom the party to proceedings has exercised the contractual advantage. The Act introduces drastic fines for both traders who exercise a contractual advantage as well as for managers or members of management bodies if they make it difficult to carry out checks or mislead the Controller. The President of the Office may impose a fine on both the manager and the member of the management body, as I said. Of course, it can take into account mitigating circumstances, and in particular the voluntary removal of the effects of the infringement or the abandonment of an unfair contract advantage practice on its own initiative. We believe that this law will force equal conditions in terms of contracts and implementation of these contracts, force the removal of pathologies such as extortion of prices, extension of payment deadlines for delivered goods, extortion of additional benefits, e.g. such as the application by trade of large-area charges for exhibition and commercial space, fees for advertising services, for the disposal of products, including logistics charges, discounts to catalogues, promotional discounts, for commercial newspapers, etc. I am absolutely convinced, and we will be working on this law, if it is the will of the House, to continue working in the committee to resolve any doubts in the relationship between suppliers and recipients, but this type of law is absolutely necessary, because in the chain from field to table, in the food production chain and in the trade in this food that Minister Babalski was talking about, agricultural producers are the link of the weakest, the food suppliers are the link of the weakest. Of course, this does not mean that we want to move away from organizing them, but in this trade agreement, those who are the weakest are taken under protection and care. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u308	Thank you very much. Mr Tadeusz Azievicz, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u309	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft law against unfair exploitation of contract advantages in the marketing of food and agricultural products, print No 790. High Seym! This project has brought back to the High House a discussion on the administrative boundaries of interference with business-to-business relations that has been underway for many years. In this case, the aim is to counter the reprehensible practices used by traders with high turnover but without a dominant position within the meaning of antitrust law in relation to weaker suppliers. The problem is not new and concerns entrepreneurs from other industries as well. Time has shown that market mechanisms do not fully discharge this tension. Currently, conflicts arising from the reprehensible use of excessive contractual advantages can be settled on the basis of a law against unfair competition. It should be noted, however, that this law allows for the enforcement of claims before civil courts, which for various reasons is cumbersome and not effective for those subject to unfair practices. That is why the proposed solution, as I understand it, provides for an administrative procedure for resolving such disputes through administrative proceedings conducted by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. The President of the OOKiK already has instruments to counter abuse of dominant positions based on the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection. However, they are not sufficient to address the problem addressed by the draft law, given that none of the major networks has a dominant position on local markets, according to the analysis of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. In this context, the introduction into the law of an infringement consisting of unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage seems to be a solution worth considering, all the more so as the legal instruments used to investigate this advantage and practices by large entities will in principle be similar to those used by the OMC in antitrust proceedings. But there are problems and questions. They concern first of all the under-defined and very widely understood concepts of contractual advantage and abuse of it. It is worth noting that there are high penalties, up to 3% of turnover, for violating these undefined rules. The next question is the question of effectiveness, how large buyers will behave after the entry into force of this regulation, whether the real big difference in the market power of the entities covered by the contract will not materialise in other pathological behaviours. The procedure provided for in the draft law itself is largely the calculator of solutions that already exist in the Competition and Consumer Protection Act. In this context, the obvious question arises as to whether the possible implementation of government-designed solutions should not be carried out by amending the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection. Why should additional legal protection apply only to the market for food and agricultural products? The authors of the first, very unfortunate part of the justification, in my view, note that substitution in the production process of good quality raw materials is worse, as well as the use of cheaper chemicals used for preservation and coloration than it results from the contract is a mere fraud, which should be exterminated regardless of the adoption or rejection of the regulation in question and should not be an argument for its adoption to any extent. The devil is in the details. The Civic Platform Club supports the referral of the project to further proceding in committees, where we will join the work on detailed records, and the final shape of the project will depend on our final position. Thank you for your attention.

We proceed with the examination of item 8 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and the Act on Income Tax on Individuals (Print No 794). ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u349	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the government amendments to the rules for the implementation of certain tasks of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, which is a novelty of the law. The draft law amending the Law of 9 May 2008 on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture is amended to: create legal opportunities for the head of the district office or the director of the regional branch in which a certain type of task has been accumulated, to use the help of employees employed in another district office or regional branch who carry out similar tasks and which at the same time can assist other offices or branches in carrying out tasks; secondly, to increase from 15 000. PLN up to 40,000. zł amount above which the consent of the Minister of Finance is required for the remission of claims; thirdly, to enable the President of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture to waive claims ex officio where no debt has been recovered as a result of winding-up or insolvency proceedings carried out, and also where there is a reasonable presumption that enforcement proceedings will not obtain an amount higher than the costs of the recovery and enforcement of claims of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture, or enforcement proceedings will prove ineffective; fourthly, the explicit regulation of the way in which the sums unduly or unduly collected by the company or members of a civil company which has been wound up; fifthly, the possibility of recovering the amounts entered in the accounts of debtors in respect of the appropriations from the budget of the European Union and the national budget by interrupting the limitation period until the end of the enforcement procedure; sixthly, the creation of a legal basis enabling deductions of claims remaining to be recovered from the Structural Funds co-financed by the European Agricultural Guidance and Guarantee Fund, from the Guidance and Guarantee Section of the European Union, and the Financial Instrument for the financing of national resources for the co-financing of the European Agricultural Fund; 24 of the Act of 9 May 2008 on the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture. AriMR county offices simultaneously support various aid schemes, i.e. payments under the direct support scheme, seven aid schemes governed by the Law of 28 February 2015 on support for rural development with the participation of the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development under the Rural Development Programme 2014-2020, aid schemes resulting from the situation on the agricultural market. In the various county offices and regional branches of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, there are different numbers and complexity of cases supported under the various support instruments, ending in principle by the competent head of the county office or the director of the regional branch of the decision on granting payments or aid, depending on the type. In order to carry out tasks smoothly, it was proposed that the cumulative tasks in each district office and regional branch should be carried out in temporarily less burdened offices or branches on the basis of separate authorisations. The proposed amendment will optimise the use of staff resources of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, thereby improving the efficiency of the implementation of the tasks by ARiMR at the level of district offices and regional branches without the need to involve additional financial resources. It is also essential to allow better and faster handling of applications under consideration by district office managers and regional branch directors. This solution will allow for the termination of their service within a similar period throughout the country. An amount of 15,000. zł, above which the approval of the Minister of Finance is required for the redemption of claims, was established in 2003, i.e. 13 years ago, and has not yet been valorized. Today we are talking about the amount of 40,000. Zł, that's the proposal. The analysis carried out of cases relating to debtors registered in the debtor's ledger, as it is also, indicates that, despite exhaustion, it is important, all the legal possibilities for the recovery of ARiMR's claims and the possession of documentation confirming the existence of statutory conditions for the redemption of those claims, the absence of the debtor's application for remission of the claim, is a fundamental matter, the absence of the debtor's request for remission prevents further proceedings from taking place. This is most often the case in cases where there is a reasonable presumption that enforcement proceedings will not be able to obtain an amount higher than the costs of enforcing the ARIMR claims or where enforcement proceedings would prove ineffective. The failure to submit an application for remission from debtors despite repeated submissions of letters containing information on the grounds, conditions, mode and form in which the application for remission is to be submitted does not allow the ARiMR to initiate the procedure for examining the application and possible remission of the claim. In practice, this means that the amount indicated in the ARiMR registers is gradually increased by the amount of interest charged on outstanding debt. It was therefore proposed that, in such situations, the President of the ARiMR should be authorised to waive the debt without the debtor's request. In turn, the increase in the possibility of recovery of the debt included in the debtor's ledger in respect of the appropriations from the budget of the European Union and the national budget is intended to ensure that the limitation period is interrupted. It shall be interrupted as a result of the enforcement measure to which the taxable person is informed, except in the cases referred to in Article. 70 § 4 Tax Order, also as a result of service to the debtor of the notice. The next proposed amendment to the Act concerns the creation of a legal basis for deductions of outstanding amounts from the Structural Funds co-financed by the measures I mentioned at the beginning. The amendments to the Act also propose that the amounts of claims remitted by ARiMR should be exempt from personal income tax. We propose that the law, if the High Sejm decides to accept it, should be sent to the committee and consequently adopted, enter into force 14 days from the date of its publication in the Official Journal of the Laws. Thank you very much, Marshal. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u361	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture and the Act on Income Tax on Individuals. The applicants in the explanatory memorandum point to several objectives of the amendment of the law. I will briefly quote the most important thing in my opinion. The first was the creation of the head of the county office or the head of the regional branch of the agency in which a certain type of task was accumulated, the possibility of legal recourse to the assistance of employees employed in another county office or regional department which carries out similar tasks and which at the same time is not unduly burdened with the performance of tasks at the same time. The proposed solution should be assessed positively. Action is needed to make the way public administration operates more flexible and to make efficient use of the potential of officials, halting the trend of bureaucracy growth observed since 1989. Secondly, the amendment provides for an increase of 15 000. PLN up to 40,000. zł the amount of the agency's claims above which the approval of the Minister of Finance is required to decide on its redemption. In addition, the proposal provides for the Agency's President to be able to waive his claims ex officio where the claim has not been recovered as a result of winding-up or insolvency proceedings carried out and where there is a reasonable presumption that enforcement proceedings will not obtain an amount higher than the costs of the agency's recovery and enforcement. These solutions, as a burden-bearing minister, should also be assessed positively, especially as the amount of 15 000. PLN has not been valued since 2003. In addition, by adding a clear regulation on how to recover amounts unduly or unduly collected from the agency by a company or members of a civil company which has been dissolved, as well as the exemption from income tax on natural persons, the amounts of claims remitted and the possibility to recover the amounts entered in the debtor's ledger in respect of the appropriations from the budget of the European Union and the national budget by interrupting the limitation period until the end of the enforcement procedure, it can be said that the proposed amendments to the law in this respect are of a comprehensive nature and are fully taken into account. An important element of the regulation is the granting of the right to the President of the Agency to waive claims against a debtor who has suffered damage caused by drought, hail, heavy rain, adverse effects of wintering, spring frosts, flooding, hurricane, lightning, landslides or avalanches within the meaning of the provisions on agricultural and livestock insurance, but excluding damage to buildings or who has become unfit to work on the agricultural holding. This is a very important provision that has been proposed in this project. These spring frosts or the negative effects of wintering are a real plague of recent years. It happens that a significant area of agricultural land grown by farmers unfortunately suffers such damage. This is certainly in line with the situation of farmers who are still affected by these negative weather phenomena. The proposed legislative changes improve the functioning of the Agency and increase the transparency of clearing between the Agency and its service providers, introducing protective measures that benefit agricultural producers who have suffered damage to crops caused by weather conditions, which is of great importance to farmers. High Seym! The Free and Solidarni Circle welcomes this draft law as a substantive project, while asking for it to be referred to further work in the parliamentary committees. Thank you very much.
