ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u401	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Civil Committee of the Legislative Initiative, I have the honour to present to you and your fellow Members a bill amending the law on the income of local government units and certain other laws, while asking for the support of this key group of teachers of more than 600 thousand cases. Our project is the result of many years of collecting difficult experiences of functioning in legal chaos, struggling with conflicting regulations and lack of elementary responsibility for the existence of the largest professional group in Poland. None of us, also sitting here in the courtroom, can imagine accepting a situation where our employer pays us much less than he should. In short, he cheats us to save on us, sometimes being forced to do so by the budgetary situation. Teachers are no exception here, but we find it difficult to accept a situation where, instead of paying us for hard work, we sometimes hear excuses or unbelievable translations, possibly comments that have nothing to do with reality. In the current legal system, however, it is possible for Parliament to determine the level of teachers' salaries, the Ministry of Education issued detailed decisions and regulations on this basis, the Minister of Finance guaranteed money for this and transferred it to local governments, and the local government spent everything on other than teachers' salaries objectives, e.g. for the construction of plumbing, road renovation or debt repayment. Teachers in protest can at best go to the head of their workplace, i.e. the head of the school or kindergarten, which can at most express regret, because it has a minimal impact on the level of the teacher's salary. The Polish Teachership Association believes that this absurd system, which has been in operation for several years, in which no one is responsible for anything and in which it is possible to steal from the money they owe, must finally be changed. We believe that enough of this sick makeshifter, it is time to make clear who are responsible for paying the teacher's benefits. We're not demanding anything out of the ordinary. As with the salary of the official, the minister, the policeman - the chief officer, the soldier - the minister of defence, and the Member - the Marshal of the Sejm, the teacher's salary must also be responsible for the specific authority of the State. We want it to be the Minister of National Education. And that's actually what our citizens' bill is all about. The decision of how much a teacher earns falls here in this courtroom. It is you who have written in the Teacher's Charter what the average teacher's salary is at each level of professional promotion: an intern's teacher, contract teacher, appointed and certified. You also set the amount of the so-called budget law every year as part of the budget law. the basic amount on which the average remuneration is calculated. You enter the amount of the education part of the general subsidy in the budget bill at the same time. And this huge, because almost PLN 42 billion, the budget column really speaks to the imagination of the public opinion, but we want to point out that these funds are almost entirely devoted to teacher salaries, which, anyway, all citizens of this country know, are not among the highest. Once you decide how much teachers will earn next year, the Minister of Education decides how to distribute the educational grant in a special regulation. This is the basis for calculating how much subsidy money is due to each school authority, mainly to municipalities and powiats. In this way, the money goes to local government accounts, which should distribute it between educational institutions in their territory, so that every principal can pay any teacher and every teacher their salary, which is compatible with them. with education, promotion, internship, number of hours of teaching, but also level of interest in and involvement in work. We're talking about motivational additions here. As you can see, the road that teachers pay is long, full of curves and bumps. No wonder, then, that along the way this and that from a huge 42 billion-dollar pot dies and does not go where it belongs. Unfortunately, this is possible because the educational subsidy is so constructed that the local government of money earmarked for educational purposes, including the salaries of teachers and the maintenance of schools, does not have to spend these funds for these purposes at all. The money from the education grant can come to any choice that has nothing to do with the education of the target. That's what happens. High Izbo! Practically all the time and throughout the country we observe phenomena that I described in the introduction, which show that the management of the educational part of the general subsidy presents many problems, many discussions and arouses many emotions in both teacher and local environments. The Polish Teachers Association is regularly informed by teachers about cases of underpaid salaries, because the mayor or uncle had another, in his opinion a more important goal: repair of the road, renovation of the office or unpaid payment in the bank. In theory, education law provided for a tool to safeguard the interests of teachers - it is the need for local authorities to report annually on the payment of secondary teachers. If, in a given municipality, the funds paid at the various degrees of promotion were lower than those recorded in the card, teachers should be given the so-called. Supplementary allowances, sometimes referred to as the 14th salary, which is incorrect and harmful to the teacher's environment. We're talking about that term. However, as several years of practice has shown, this is not an effective and fair mechanism. Firstly, local authorities often pay underpaid salaries despite the obligation to report, and then delay paying compensation, i.e. that the mechanism is ineffective and poorly self-government disciplines. Secondly, compensation is not always shared fairly and goes to those to whom it should go. The Polish Teachership Association has repeatedly drawn attention to this element. Finally, thirdly, if any of the Members of Parliament would like to receive a salary of 10 and 15% underpaid for 12 months Despite the knowledge that the Sejm Office next year might even out these shortcomings? I don't think so. And teachers have to, but they don't want to accept it either. It is not just us, representatives of the Polish Teachers' Association, who are talking about irregularities at the payment of teachers' salaries and compensations. Our observations are also confirmed by numerous institutions, such as regional accounting chambers, which regularly check the legality of public spending by local governments. According to the latest report of the Regional Financial Chambers for 2015, irregularities in the determination and payment of one-off supplementary allowances for teachers cover every fifth teacher, so every fifth... sorry, every fifth local authority and it is 20% units running schools in our country, which means that teachers employed in these units of local government have to deal with these shortcomings that I have said about - underwage or waiting for additional allowances to be paid. And here are some examples. In the municipality of Dubiecko, Podkarpackie Voivodeship, in 2014 no one-off supplementary allowance was paid to teachers employed in educational institutions for 2013 in a total amount of over 352 thousand. zł. In the municipality of Kielce, Świętokrzyskie Voivodeship, irregularities were found in the calculation and payment of one-off supplementary allowances for teachers. The self-government entered incorrect data on teachers at various grades of promotion in the average payment report, for which the teachers were paid by the diplomat because the supplementary allowance due for the years 2013 and 2014 was reduced by a total amount of PLN 2,702 861. The municipality of Poraj, the Śląskie voivodship, has not been able to transfer money to schools for supplementary allowances in the short term, and therefore schools could not pay one-off allowances for 2012 and 2013. The appropriations for this purpose have only been transferred by officials on 14 February 2013 and 12 February 2014. In the RIO report For 2014, you can read, quote: In addition, in 2014 funds for expenditure for educational units were given in insufficient amounts, by an amount of 1168 thousand. As at 30 June 2014 and 789 thousand. as at 30 September 2014. End of quote. Controllers often encountered cases of funding by local governments from money allocated to schools, teachers of other tasks which have little to do with the education of children. In the municipality of Krypno, the payment of the educational subsidy for January 2014 was intended to finance the hole in the 2013 budget. It was a total of 254 thousand, because the municipality was in debt not, as planned, at 327 thousand, but at an amount of 0.5 million more. In the RIO report stressed, I quote: the difference was financed by spending all the funds remaining on the budget account, including the instalment of the educational part of the general subsidy for 2014, and the debt to the budget account of 400 000. Despite the lack of grounds in the budget resolution to increase the debt in 2013 by this amount to finance the deficit. End of quote. This is why the current Minister of National Education, when asked for an opinion on the initiative of the Polish Teachers Association, says that banks are against it because banks have an interest in it. The debted district of Świebodziński wanted to finance a hole in the budget caused by an out-of-court settlement by means of a loan from the state budget. To this end, he put in place a programme of prudential measures, which means that he cut expenditure on education. Further cases of regional irregularities are described in the report by the Boards of Auditors in the Debt of local government units. Compliance with statutory debt limits and its repayment. Among other things, she found herself in it. the case of the municipality Zbójna, which financed the budget expenditure in 2012. As a result, an expenditure plan was drawn up for 2013, in which revenue was overestimated by more than 250 000. zł. Millowka County performed a similar operation twice. The first time she borrowed more than PLN 0.5 million from subsidies for 2012 and in 2012 more than 790 thousand. zł, already with subsidies for 2013. The reason for such an operation was the difficult financial situation of the municipality. Such operations overwhelmed the real income of the municipality, which in turn allowed the local authorities to borrow higher loans and larger loans. Creative accounting was allowed not only by small towns but also by larger cities. For example, the Siedlce took out almost PLN 0.5 million from the educational grant for 2012 and allocated it to finance the 2011 budget hole. Moreover, the city authorities did so without taking into account the above operation in the accounting and reporting records. According to the Regional Audit Chamber, financing a hole in the government budget or various expenditures e.g. education subsidies are a worrying phenomenon. The report stresses: not all monetary resources available to the local government can be used to finance planned expenditure or budget expenditure. Of course, not in every municipality and not in every county there are such irregularities. Most of the leading bodies - I repeat, most of the leading bodies - try to pay teachers honestly, within time and legally, and they deserve the words of great appreciation, because they sometimes do so at the expense of huge sacrifices. However, the system, as I have shown earlier, is so complicated, so multi-stage and raises problems also for local governments. Suffice it to say that additional expenditure must pay 3/4 of the local government every year. Ladies and gentlemen, it's not a mistake how many dishonest local governments there are, it's a problem of a systemic nature. Therefore, we believe that this complex, chaotic, unfair and abusive system needs to be simplified and basically civilised and unambiguously pointed out that the government and the Minister for National Education, not local governments, are responsible for the payment of teachers' salaries. To this end, we believe that three laws should be amended: the law on the income of local government units, the law on the education system and the Act on the Teacher Charter. It's about the rules on liability for the payment of teachers' salaries. In the Education Act, this means that it is necessary to rewrite the article. Article 5a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 3 to read as follows: The measures necessary for the implementation of the educational tasks referred to in paragraph. 2 and 2b, including for the maintenance of schools and institutions, are guaranteed in the income of local government units. The resources necessary for the remuneration of teachers in institutions run by local government units shall be transferred by the State through a targeted grant. The Act on the Income of Local Government Units would appear, among others. Article 2 Article 42 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 point 5c, according to which the average salaries of teachers, together with the wage-based write-off funds for the social benefit fund, would be financed by the State in the form of a special subsidy. In addition, the Minister of Education would have the competence to determine annually, by means of a regulation, the amount of the special-purpose grant for the remuneration of teachers, the manner and modalities of granting this special-purpose grant, and to allocate and transfer it to local authorities. Self-governments would only become a cashier paying the teacher a salary according to this solution. The responsibility for salary would fall directly to the Minister of National Education. We are also convinced that the Teacher's Charter needs to be amended. Article 30 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 8, which would include the principle that the funds necessary for the remuneration of teachers, together with wage derivatives, the write-down funds for the occupational welfare fund, the funding for the professional training of teachers, including methodological advice, shall be provided by the State by means of a targeted grant. All these changes would have one objective: to restore the State's responsibility for maintaining the dignity of the teaching profession. Yes, High Izbo, it's about dignity over 640 thousand. teachers and teachers as well as the daily existence of them and their loved ones. So that every teacher doesn't have to wonder what his hard-earned salary will go for next month: to support his family, or maybe to support the local authorities. We are convinced that our concept, our proposal is beneficial not only for teachers, but also for the Polish state. The money that you want to spend on education and the future of the youngest generation will go where it should go and not spread out to different sides. Fair local authorities will also benefit from our proposal. A difficult and complex duty to care for the level of teachers' salaries will be removed. After the adoption by the High House of our bill, this task will rest on the shoulders of government and administration in the form of the Ministry of National Education. High Izbo! The solutions we have entered into the civil bill are not some of our own novel ideas. These are standard solutions in most European countries. With any difference in how teachers are employed in other countries of our continent, each country is trying to maintain control over teachers' salaries. In Lithuania, as in Poland, municipalities are the governing body of schools. However, the money for their maintenance is provided by the government, using here, as is our case, a special algorithm. Then the money is transferred to the municipalities' accounts, only not in the form of subsidies, but in the form of grants. The money received must be spent on tasks commissioned by the Lithuanian Ministry of National Education, as well as on the salaries of teachers and other school staff, the purchase of textbooks and teaching aids, as well as the professional improvement of teachers. In Luxembourg educational tasks were divided among others. between central authorities and local authorities. The government deals with e.g. teaching programmes, manuals, framework curricula, teacher training, as well as the budget of primary schools, including the remuneration of teachers employed and employed in those schools. Teachers are directly employed by the Luxembourg Ministry of Education and then posted to work in a specific municipality. On the other hand, the municipality directly supervises the work of schools, recruits pupils to individual classes and evaluates them. In Malta, teachers working in public schools, with two thirds of pupils, are public officials. The Ministry of Education is responsible for practically all important aspects of school activities, including administration, organisation and financing. A teacher with the status of a public official is a European standard. This is the case in Slovenia, for example, where local authorities are responsible only for the administration of kindergartens and primary schools. The same is true in Italy, where the teacher is a public official and the Minister of National Education and representatives of the Ministry in the regions are responsible for education. Also in most German states, teachers are state officials employed by local national ministries of national education. As you go further west, you can look at France, which, according to the Republican tradition, takes close care of schools, including in financial terms. The local government is responsible for the construction and maintenance of public school buildings, while the central government is responsible for education policy, teaching staff and school organisation, including an adequate number of employees. In Latvia, salaries of public school teachers are paid from the state budget, although they are not public officials. Teachers employ schools that impose specific responsibilities on teachers. In Austria, teachers are employed by the Federal Government or the State Government and treated as state officials. It is the government that is responsible for the functioning of the education system, including among others. for the organisation of schools and the remuneration and pension rights. Although in Ireland primary and secondary schools are mostly privately owned, mainly by religious communities, the state is borne by, among others. the cost of teachers' salaries. Technically, teachers employ a school, but the salary money comes from the state budget. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, If our citizens' project succeeds through parliament, schools will have a really good change, which I sincerely wish you, but also to my colleagues. I would also like to thank everyone on this occasion more than 340 thousand. teachers and educational staff who have signed the citizens' initiative, which I am honoured to present to you today. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u443	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Applicant's Representatives! I have the honour to present to the High House the position of the government in relation to the citizens' draft law amending the law on the income of local government units and certain other laws contained in print No 732. The draft law amending the law on the income of local government units and certain other laws, drawn up by the Civic Committee of the Legislative Initiative, concerns changes in the article. 28 and Article 28 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). 42 Law on the Income of Local Government Units, Art. 5a Act on the Education System and Art. 30th Teacher Card Act. In the current legal state, the way and sources of funding for educational tasks are laid down by the Law on the Education System and the Teacher Charter. According to art. Article 5a (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: The 3th Education Act provides the means necessary for the performance of educational tasks, including the remuneration of teachers and the maintenance of schools and institutions, are guaranteed in the income of local government units. In addition, in accordance with Article. Article 30 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 8 The Teacher Charter Law measures necessary for the average salaries of teachers together with wage derivatives, the write-down funds for the occupational welfare fund and the funding for the professional training of teachers, taking into account methodological advice, are guaranteed by the State in the income of local government units. Revenues of local government units pursuant to Art. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Constitutions are own income, general subsidies and special subsidies from the state budget. One of the revenues of local government units is the educational part of the general subsidy. In accordance with the Article. 27 and Article 27 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers pursuant to Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 (OJ L 298, 26.10.2012, p. 1). The 28th Act on the Income of Local Government Units The size of the The amount allocated to the education part of the general subsidy for all units of local government shall be set at the total amount of the education part of the general subsidy, not less than that adopted in the budget law in the base year, adjusted by the amount of other expenditure for the modification of the educational tasks carried out. The distribution of part of the general subsidy between municipalities, powiats and local governments of voivodships - after deduction of the statutory reserve - shall be carried out in a manner determined by the Minister responsible for education and education in a regulation issued pursuant to Article. Article 28 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6 Act on the income of local government units. The citizens' bill envisages changing the system of financing teachers' salaries to a system based entirely on a targeted grant from the state budget. To this end, the project promoters propose to add to the catalogue of their own tasks, for which these entities may receive special subsidies from the State budget, the payment of teachers' salaries. The Constitution requires the legislator to maintain the proportion between the size of the income of the JST and the extent of their educational changes. By establishing the power of the local government to act on the basis of its own budget and on its own behalf, the basic law establishes at the same time a generalised source of coverage for the financing of tasks relating to the implementation of the laws, leaving the key decisions to the authorities constituting the local government units. There is one generalised source of funding for the local government's own tasks - these are total revenue: own income, subsidies, grants. As indicated above, the revenues of local government units are precisely these sources. This means that both the basic law and the ordinary law do not create legal solutions that allow certain sources of income to be allocated to specific expenditures in terms of education. The method of financing educational tasks is linked to the freedom of local self-government units in shaping educational policy in their territory, including among others. the right to decide on the level of employment in their schools, as well as the level of certain allowances for teachers' salaries. Local government units, when deciding on education, have an impact on the level of funding of tasks resulting from their decisions. The State budget may not cover the costs of the tasks set out in the decisions of the local authorities. The acquisition of remuneration by the central administration would have to be linked to the acquisition of control over many educational decisions, which would result in the need to develop educational standards. At the same time, such an action would deprive the local government of the freedom to carry out its own tasks and would prevent policy-making taking into account local circumstances and needs. Taking over the funding of teachers' salaries by the central administration while maintaining the local authorities' competence to decide on the level of employment and on the level of certain wage allowances could lead to an uncontrolled increase in the employment of teachers in schools and, consequently, to a huge and unpredictable burden on the state budget. In addition, the government recognises project irregularities, serious substantive errors of justification and legislative errors. We note the overestimation of about PLN 2 billion of the financial impact of the proposed changes by indicating excessive data on the number of teachers' posts, the underestimation of the amount of the rural allowance, the overestimation of the impact of the occupational social benefit fund, the miscalculation of funds for the professional improvement of teachers, the failure to include in the calculation of funds for the payment of income from salaries, the failure to include in the calculation of funds transferred by local government units above the average remuneration resulting from Article. 30th Teacher Card Act. In addition, there is no specific guidance on the content of the regulation to which the project promoters have authorised the Minister responsible for Education and Education. The draft law does not specify the rules according to which the allocation of the funds of the special subsidy is to be made. At the same time, it should be noted that the introduction of the proposed system of funding for education in the form of targeted grants from the state budget would have to entail a significant administrative burden on the part of the school authorities and institutions. Such a system raises the issue of not only the calculation of grants and the associated formalities by imposing further administrative obligations, but also the question of clearing grants and controlling the use of grants, which would require an additional, substantial amount of work and would therefore involve the extension of the bureaucratic apparatus and additional administrative financial burdens. The change in the system of financing the remuneration of teachers, including the transfer of huge resources to local governments in the form of a targeted grant, would not be in line with the European Charter of Local Self-Government, ratified by Poland in 1994, which indicates that special grants should be limited from the sources of funding for local self-government units. In the legal financial literature, it is stressed that the state budget special grants for the financing of current own tasks should play a limited role among the sources of revenue of the local government unit. The main justification for the proposed change in the system of funding educational tasks was the problem of school closures and their transfer to other entities in order to circumvent the provisions of the Teacher Card Act. It is a fact that in recent years some local government units have liquidated their schools, while including schools run by other entities into the public school network or agreeing to the functioning of non-public schools established in place of closed-down local schools. In order to prevent these actions, in the draft amendment of the Law on the system of education prepared in agreement with the Ministry of National Education, from 23 January, the condition for the liquidation of a local public school is to pass, to obtain a positive opinion of the curator issued by order. This means that it is impossible to close the school without the guardian's positive opinion. These solutions are in line with the request formulated by the Polish Teachers Association to strengthen the role of education curator in terms of its impact on the implementation of the compulsory task of self-governments associated with the running of kindergartens, schools and institutions. In addition, the Law of 23 June 2016 amending the Law on the Education System and certain other laws introduced amendments to local government units which all their self-government schools have provided for the conduct of natural or legal persons regarding the payment of grants to these institutions in the amount of expenses per pupil of the type and type of school run by the nearest unit and not in the amount of grants received. The regulations of 23 June 2016 were absolutely discouraged by local governments, which pass on local schools, all they have in their territory. Minister of National Education on 27 June this year. in Toruń, during a conference summarizing the nationwide debate on the school system. Parents. Teacher - A good change has presented comprehensive plans for changes in the area of education. The government recognises the need to analyse and discuss with interested environments, in particular representatives of teachers' trade unions and representatives of local government units, detailed solutions to issues related to the professional status of teachers, including issues related inter alia. with employment conditions, qualifications, pay and promotion systems, working time, and discussions on financial support for small schools. Accordingly, the Minister for National Education, in the framework of the work of the Social Dialogue Council, called for the establishment of a professional team for education. The aim of the team will be to develop together the best possible solutions, suitable for contemporary economic and social conditions, and at the same time to motivate teachers to improve their own workshop to improve the quality of education and education according to the needs of the young generation. In conclusion, I would like to say that there is one general source of funding for the tasks of local self-government units - these are total revenue. The method of financing educational tasks is linked to the freedom of local self-government units in shaping educational policy in their territory. Local government units, when deciding on education, have an impact on the level of funding of tasks resulting from their decisions. The State budget may not cover the costs of the tasks set out in the decisions of the local authorities. The acquisition of remuneration by the central administration would have to be linked to the acquisition of control over a number of educational decisions, which would result in a restriction on the discretion of local authorities. Such actions would deprive the local government of freedom, would prevent policy-making taking into account local circumstances and needs. In view of the above, the government is negative about the citizens' draft law amending the law on the income of local government units and certain other laws. During the debate on the draft at this point of the Sejm, most of the questions raised by your Members were addressed to the Minister for National Education. I understand that in the High House there is a great need to talk about the funding of education, the professional status of teachers, the position of teacher, there is a need to address this important problem, which is the income of local government units in terms of running, maintaining schools, kindergartens and institutions. I don't think this is the time or place to answer the question of whether the Minister expects an increase in subsidies. It's not that simple, ladies and gentlemen. We need to talk about this. I hope that a trilateral table will be set up, where trade unions and local government and the Minister of Education must be located, because only in such a group of developed solutions can be leading, can be implemented and in agreement, so to speak, presented in the High House. Of course we are ready to talk, there are other forms, other points of work of the Sejm, there are current information, questions on current issues, we are also at your disposal. In connection with your club speeches, I assume that the citizens' project will also be addressed to the committee. We are also ready to talk, to analyze and really very much to say that we are looking for the best solutions for financing, but also for the functioning of education in Poland. Thank you. The 2015 NIK activity report, including the report, discusses the most important of 144 planned audits, the 27 most important selected ad hoc controls. They are presented in Chapter III of the report entitled "The implementation of the State's tasks in the light of NIK's audit and in Chapter II : Control activities, Chapter IV: Risks of corruption in the light of the audit results. I am talking about this document because this country has the greatest impact on its shape, and we also discussed it at the meeting of the Committee on State Control, and it is according to previous recommendations that Members' requests are the final form of the annual report. Of course, we are open to the development of this document in line with the requests and expectations of the ladies and gentlemen. Ladies and gentlemen! In 2015, as I said, the House carried out a series of audits, which met with great interest from ladies and gentlemen, industry experts, media and public opinion. The House's activity was particularly focused on low quality public services, the lack of rationalisation of public spending, often low competitiveness and innovation of our economy, sometimes ineffective or inefficient in combating socio-demographic changes, the lack of effective action to improve the quality of the law and its enforcement in some cases. In particular, we have highlighted the role of guardian of public funds in the role of the Chamber as legal guardian, i.e. legal, economic, targeted and reliable use of public funds, particularly in areas important for the functioning of the State, i.e. in areas relating to public finances, economy, security, the functioning of public administration, agriculture, environmental protection, culture and education, and social and health security. Of course, the issue of public finances, including the revenue and effectiveness of the authorities responsible for this area, remains of particular interest to NIK. As part of the control activities of the NIK, we took into account, among others, the checks carried out last year. matters relating to the settlement of corporate income tax by entities with foreign capital or the actions of the Minister of Finance aimed at reducing the transfer of income for reasons mainly or exclusively of tax reasons, i.e. so-called. tax optimisation. At this point, if you will allow me, I have selected some of the most important checks from last year, and it is not possible at this moment not to start with checks on the expenditure of public funds or checks on the tax system of the State. Such control was the control of the supervision of tax authorities and tax authorities over the regularity of the accounts with the budget of the State of entities with foreign capital. NIK concluded that often audited tax offices and tax control offices did not carry out sufficient and effective measures to prevent tax evasion by entities with foreign capital through the so-called. transfer of revenue outside the Polish tax system. The authorities under the Minister of Finance, in line with the Minister's recommendations, focused in particular on combating irregularities in the area of goods and services tax, VAT. During the period considered, the Minister of Finance often did not have an analysis of the magnitude of the spread of the tax base and the transfer of income between foreign capital entities and effective legal tools ensuring the tightness of the tax system in the context of such entities. We are pleased to note that in 2014 work on sealing the Polish tax system was started and further accelerated, and to develop the approach of entities subordinate to the finance minister to the control of revenue transfer entities. The Minister of Finance identified the risks associated with the income tax settlement of foreign capital entities and prepared legislative changes to eliminate the aggressive tax planning mechanism. The changes aimed at reducing the tax base in income tax and income transfer outside the Polish tax system were consistent with the solutions recommended by the bodies of the European Union and the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development. The tax authorities in the assessment of the House have not always been effectively prepared to check the regularity of the accounts with the budget of the State of entities with foreign capital. Few checks carried out to verify the legality of foreign transfers of income were too small in relation to needs. In addition to the insufficient often prepared tax authorities and tax authorities to carry out these checks, too few planned checks were affected by the time constraints resulting from the Law on the Freedom of Business and the focus of the Finance Minister's authorities on combating irregularities in the tax on goods and services. In the report of this audit, NIK indicated that in the Polish tax system there is no general clause against tax avoidance, and in many countries this solution has been in place and has been in operation for years. According to the House, the absence of this clause under applicable law is an obstacle to ensuring the integrity of the Polish tax system. Business operators operating in many countries to reduce their tax obligations are often willing to apply the so-called. aggressive tax planning and this phenomenon contributes to the reduction of the national tax base, resulting in a reduction in tax revenue. This also allows international operators to gain an advantage over local businesses and leads to harmful competition, as tax burdens on local operators are therefore higher than those on international operators. Sealing the tax system to prevent tax avoidance by entities operating in many countries, including the introduction of a general anti-tax avoidance clause, should help to reduce unfair competition from aggressive tax planning operators and, as a result, limit the concealment of part of their activities by legally operating companies precisely because of unfair competition. In our speech to the Minister of Finance, we have pointed out that effective detection and combating of such abuses and the protection of the tax base require decisive action at central level, and we have made a number of recommendations and recommendations to achieve this objective. We are pleased that these proposals are being implemented in a successive manner. Another extremely important control in this area was the control on the prevention of the placing on the market of invoices documenting fictitious activities. In this respect, the NIK assessed as ineffective the activities of the Minister of Finance. The tax authorities we are investigating, as recommended by the Minister of Finance, focused their efforts on combating irregularities in the tax on goods and services. The controls included entities with a high risk of irregularities and the checks ended with findings of irregularities. Controlled tax offices carried out security and enforcement procedures fairly, in accordance with the applicable procedure - I stress this very strongly - unfortunately, these actions were mostly ineffective. From entities involved in fraudulent activities, mostly so-called. pole companies were not enforced as a result of tax controls. In most cases, the tax authorities did not determine the actual tax fraud organisers either. I would, of course, like to stress very strongly that this problem is a problem facing all the countries in which the so-called one operates. VAT, however, we must honestly say that in Poland during the period considered, i.e. In 2014 and 2015, the volume of fiscal controls detected by the tax authorities increased, mainly in quota terms, which obviously also resulted in an increase in the amount of tax applied. Thus, in 2013 tax authorities detected fictitious invoices for the amount of PLN 19 700 million, a year later - 33 billion, in the first half of 2015 already for the amount of 28 billion, and in the whole year for the amount of almost PLN 82 billion. On the basis of the article. 108 of the Goods and Services Tax Act, the tax authorities of the tax authorities measured the tax due in amounts ranging from 2300 million in 2013 to almost 5 billion in the first half of 2015. Unfortunately, payments to the State budget in 2013 and the first half of 2015 amounted to only PLN 162 million, or 1,3%. the amounts resulting from the decisions of the dimensional tax authorities. It must be said that the low efficiency in recovering the tax due has, of course, led to a sharp increase in the backlog of VAT from decisions taken pursuant to Article. 108 - they increased from 5 billion in 2013 to over 14 billion at the end of June 2015. In the report from this audit We have pointed out that the possibilities for recovery of these amounts are small and we have proposed mechanisms to seal the tax system on areas of economic activity with particular risks. The system solutions we have proposed, among others. The reverse charge mechanism and the institution of joint and several liability of the buyer, and organisational changes have unfortunately not always been sufficient to improve the effectiveness of the fight against these frauds, although we note that after this report, in the previous years, also last year, and this year, activities are being carried out in the Ministry of Finance to seal this tax system. An important control for us was the inspection of the 15 largest privatisation transactions from 2012 to 2014. In the course of these 15 privatisations, the State Treasury received more than 90% Total revenues from privatisation during this period. During study 15 privatisation processes in case 11 we did not find significant irregularities, in case of four such irregularities we found. These were privatisation processes concerning sales of 85% shares of the Coal Mine of the Adams, sales 37.9% Ciech SA shares on the Stock Exchange in response to a call for sale, sales more than 86% shares of Zakłady Górniczo-Hutnicze Bolesław SA and sales 85% shares of the furniture company Emilia SA. A very important area of control for us - now I am closing the area concerning public finance issues - is the controls that concern the various areas of the state's functioning, especially those concerning so-called controls. socially sensitive areas. Undoubtedly, these were, are, and will be, the sphere of education. Each year, more than one million pupils and graduates of primary schools, middle schools and upper secondary schools take the exams - their outcome increasingly affects the further educational path - so-called. external examinations. Exams enabling students to check their achievements at the end of the different stages of teaching, while ensuring comparability of results at national and local level in a given year, of course, is absolutely fundamental. In view of the above, NIK assessed the functioning of the so-called. the external examination system in education, in particular external examinations as a mechanism for verifying the knowledge and skills of pupils, the efficiency of the Central Examination Commission in Warsaw and the district examination committees, the way and scope of using external examinations to improve the quality of education at the various levels of education management. Unfortunately, this assessment was not successful. The audit has shown that the functioning system of external examinations requires corrective action, in particular in the field of preparation of examination tests and equal treatment for examination work. The control mechanisms built into the system were not fully effective. In the case of 1/4 of the work verified at the applicant's request, it was necessary to change the results of the examinations and to exchange the certificate following that amendment. Nor was there a rule of re-checking all the work assessed by examiners who made mistakes - we are talking about examiners who made mistakes earlier in checking the work. Re-verification of their knowledge should be a permanent element. The external examination system established 15 years ago since 2009 has not been subject to mandatory annual evaluation by the Central Examination Board. Hence our proposal for changes in the context of the preparation of tests. Here we can say that these changes - also after our control - are gradually implemented, which we note with great satisfaction, and we must remember that we are talking about a very large number of work that is being tested in this way every year. The examination work, for example, was checked by examiners entered in the records kept by the district examination committees; we are talking together, on a yearly basis, about 180 thousand. That kind of work. In order to reduce the risk of error, part of the work, up to 10%, was re-evaluated. The work was also reviewed at the request of the applicants. A worrying phenomenon during the period considered was a large number of examinations whose verification at the applicant's request led to a change in the results and exchange of certificates. It was in the carefully tested group. 26%. However, all the work assessed by this examiner, which made a mistake, has not been reviewed. In our view, this meant a weakness in the control mechanisms of the Central Examination Board. The system of external examinations has not been evaluated as comprehensively since 1999 as the one we subjected to in the course of this review of the House. The control regarding the fundamental problems of the Polish road network was important for us due to the size of the public funds involved. Of course, such a fundamental problem in the Polish road network is the lack of a full, sufficient number of motorways and high-speed roads. This is an essential factor in reducing the availability of the country's communication. Of course, this has been radically improved in recent years. We, as part of our audit, audited the activities of the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways to ensure the quality of road works. We have often heard in various reports of reported problems with the implementation of road investments. They focused mainly on their short-term implementation and low quality of the works carried out. This information showed that the general management selected designers, contractors and entities responsible for managing and exercising investor supervision most often on the basis of the lowest price criterion. On this basis, the selection of participants in the construction process sometimes, or even often, did not help to ensure the required quality of road works, as evidenced by cases of removal of defects or adjustments of roads already made. The NIK audit showed that the main problem related to inadequate quality of the works carried out has for many years been the ineffective investor supervision of the investments carried out by GDDKiA. We are talking about supervision both during the preparation of project documentation and during the execution of investment tasks, which later often translated into lower than we expected, the quality of the investment work carried out, as well as the increase in their costs and the extension of the construction time. In particular, the assessment of the NIK GDDKiA was not adequately prepared to carry out the tasks resulting from the 2008-2012 national road construction programme. Thus: no fully effective mechanisms have been put in place to ensure that road works are of adequate quality - more than 70% of the road sections examined by the House have not achieved the parameters set out in the so-called. detailed technical specifications, of which 50% the audited investments of the irregularities concerned the bituminous masses carried out; close to 40% Roads put into service have not been subject to quality monitoring of works; road laboratories GDDKiA were not sufficiently equipped with the research equipment necessary to assess the quality of materials and work technology. But I would also like to convey very important information here: our control has also led to the fact that today all the GDDKiA laboratories - all 16 laboratories - have been accredited, have all the equipment needed to carry out this type of research. This is very good information also in the context of the new budgetary perspective and subsequent infrastructure investments that are planned in our country. The audit on the implementation of cyberspace protection tasks of the Republic of Poland was extremely important from the point of view of the House of Control announced at that time, which we are talking about in the report. The Chamber stated that security in cyberspace is not properly protected in Poland. Crisis response procedures have not been prepared and cyberspace protection activities have been conducted in a distributed manner without a coherent system vision. They often referred to an ad hoc, limited response to current events and a passive expectation of EU regulation. In the NIK's assessment, insufficient involvement of the public administration was an important factor in the implementation of these tasks in the area of cybersecurity, as well as disputes between the various authorities as to who is responsible for this area. There is also a need for better coordination between the authorities and institutions involved in national ICT security. In the opinion of the House, most of the state-controlled entities were still not fully aware of the tasks of the institution related to the protection of cyberspace arising, inter alia. with the management of crisis management, IT or communications. State security has often been seen in a conventional way - mainly through the implementation of physical protection measures - without taking into account technological developments and increased threats to cyberspace. In the House's view, there is a need to develop a system of financing for cybersecurity measures, to allocate additional resources and to allocate more resources, appropriate to the scale of the challenges. I would like to stress very strongly that in this information, which I am presenting - a few more checks let me say - of course, in a special way I am pointing out those areas that require some corrective action. We must remember that exactly about half of the audit ends with a positive summary of the institution's activities, but these are not checks that are of particular interest, especially where we have said that some institution, central office or other organisational units are functioning properly. I am talking about this because, for natural reasons, what I am presenting now shows in a special way those areas where activity is required, whether by the executive authority or by the legislative authority, in the sense of making changes. Undoubtedly, such an important control was also the control regarding the proper functioning of the system of ensuring the security of the state and citizens in connection with the manufacture, transport, storage and use of explosives for civil use. The audit has shown that a system designed to minimise the risks associated with the manufacture, movement, use of explosives for civil use does not ensure the achievement of the objective of ensuring the security of the state and citizens. This is due both to the incorrect achievement of these objectives by the administrative bodies set up to ensure this security and to the changes made by the legislator. I am talking about changes at the level of legislative solutions, which today do not sufficiently take into account the risk of these explosives entering the hands of unauthorised persons. The analysis carried out by the Chamber of Commerce of the risks arising from the presence of explosives in circulation within the country indicates that a coherent and well-functioning system capable of preventing unauthorised entry is necessary in order to reduce this risk. The House also drew attention to the significant differences in the existing legal regulations in the requirements imposed on companies applying for a licence or a permit and to those who have access to explosives. These differences from the point of view of the security of the state and citizens do not have reasonable justification in our assessment, since the risk of misuse of these materials is at the same level in all these cases. Health inspections are always extremely important for us. Such control during the period I report is the health check on the elderly. In the report from this audit we noted that Poland must develop a coherent, comprehensive system of medical care for elderly patients. There are no geriatrics, specialist clinics and hospital departments. Our analysis shows that the geriatrics in the elderly are working better, healthier, and that their treatment costs less than those treated by specialists in other areas. In this connection, the House pointed out the need for the Minister of Health to introduce a full health care system for the elderly. In the opinion of the House, healthcare addressed to these persons should be based on the assumptions set out in the programme of long-term senior policy in Poland adopted for 2014 and-2020, and should take into account the conclusions of the audit. This was the case, among others. the valuation of geriatric benefits. In the opinion of the House, this should be designed so that financial conditions enable the creation of new geriatric facilities, especially since, as I said, this treatment is not only more effective from the point of view of the elderly, but also cheaper than treatment in other branches. The monitoring of the functioning of the preventive vaccination system was very interesting. The introduction of mandatory preventive vaccinations has contributed to reducing the incidence of many infectious diseases, some of which have been completely eliminated. Here, an extremely interesting issue is the increasing number of parents' refusals in the context of compulsory vaccination and therefore the increasing number of children not vaccinated. In 2012, it was 4700 children, and in 2014 it was more than 12 thousand. During this inspection we also pointed out that it has not been possible for several years to extend and modernise the mandatory vaccination catalogue. We have pointed out the need for financial resources to be earmarked to address this problem. Another extremely important control - I give some examples in various areas - was the control of the water protection of the river Bug against pollution. The audit was so interesting that we conducted it at the same time as the Audit Chamber of Ukraine and the Belarus Audit Office. The results were so interesting that it turned out that despite the radical improvement of the environmental infrastructure on the Polish side, there was no radical improvement in the issue of pollution of the Bug river. In the course of this audit, we have revealed that the responsibility for this really needs to be borne by the Ukrainian partner, and in particular the lack of waste water treatment, which is dumped from the Lviv agglomeration. I also give you this as an example. These sewers that are dumped into the Bug River, then through the Bug River to the Vistula River and later into the Baltic Sea, are those that are particularly responsible for such and not other parameters of the Baltic Sea's purity. Here, too, we have presented a whole range of proposals, as well as proposals that we have addressed to the European institutions in the context of, for example. consider special financing of the construction of the treatment plant for the city of Lviv. Today, the Ukrainian State is unable to allocate funds for this purpose, even if due to the situation at its eastern border. Such a social control was an extremely important control regarding the provision of public services to sign language speakers. The Chamber carried out this inspection in as many as 60 public administration offices from several voivodships. We found that the level of implementation of the law by the audited entities after almost 2 years of validity was very low. There were still areas requiring adaptation to the specific needs of people with hearing disabilities, even in those entities that took action to implement the Sign Language Act. The results of our audit showed that audited public service providers were often not prepared to interact with sign language users. In the period from the entry into force of the Sign Language Act, i.e. from 1 April 2012, until the start of the audit by us, i.e. until August 2014, many institutions have not, despite a clear statutory obligation, taken sufficient steps to ensure that the obligations set out in that Act are effectively fulfilled. These offices did not give sign language users access to information they understood. Of course, the controls concerning the spending of funds in the Treasury companies were also important for us. I'm gonna call in one control here. It was an audit concerning the expenses of PKP Group companies for consulting, consulting and expert services. In this respect, we have negatively assessed the activities of the PKP Group companies, in particular because in our assessment of organisational and procedural solutions in companies, as well as the practice used did not constitute mechanisms to minimise the risk of non-targeted, inefficient spending of funds on advisory and expert services. Internal regulations did not oblige the services to analyse the real needs and benefits of the company, as well as to assess the value of the contract. Between 2012 and 2014, 1029 contracts for consultancy and expert services with natural, legal and organisational entities with no legal personality were concluded in these four companies under scrutiny, for which PLN 170 million was spent. In our view, these orders often duplicated the competences, responsibilities of individual organisational units within the company itself. Often, in the case of the award of contracts for these advisory and expert services, the procedures did not require the carrying out and documenting of an assessment of the appropriateness of undertaking these obligations, including an analysis of the needs and costs and benefits arising from such contracts of expert services. Of course, we have made appropriate proposals to the Minister of Treasury or other ministers overseeing the various institutions for change. And it's just a password. Committee on Constitutional Responsibility - there were two abstentions here because there were not even abstentions in all other committees. Social Policy Committee - report adopted. Committee on Rules of Procedure, Parliamentary and Immunity Affairs - report adopted. Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development - report adopted. Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy - report adopted. Committee on Foreign Affairs - report adopted. In all these committees, it was a unanimous party. The Committee on Justice and Human Rights - I can only state the number: 19 votes in favour with no votes against and abstentions. Legislative Commission - Report adopted without comments. Health Committee - report accepted. I stress this because, according to the procedure, the Marshal of the Sejm sent this report to these committees earlier. The only committee that has unanimously adopted a resolution presenting an opinion on failure to adopt a report is the Commission for State Control. Finally, if you don't mind, I'd like to say that this is a surprising situation for us, of course. This is a surprising situation for us, because it seemed that this partner, with whom in a special way or with whom most knows about the NIK reports, because he usually deals with them, and I remind you that of course we are talking about the report on the House's activities, is the Commission for State Control, but maybe there is no justification in the text of the committee's position for the negative assessment. As I said, it differs in one word from earlier passed resolutions with positive opinions. Perhaps the question should be asked: Why were no arguments put forward in the resolution of the Commission for State Control which was adopted? I know why, and you know why. Because there is no such argument or such justification. An attempt not to accept an activity report contrary to substantive arguments, contrary to logic, can only be regarded as an attempt to involve the House in a political game, which I cannot and do not agree with. That is why I have presented you today with an activity report, and I ask you to accept it, with the assurance of a well-managed work done by the staff of the Supreme Auditorium, which I thank once again for the work that has been done. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u448	Thank you, Mr. President. I ask Mr Tadeusz Dziuba to present the opinion of the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u449	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! Before I refer to the committee's position extensively discussed at the meeting mentioned by the President, I would say that it was not an intention in the last part of the speech of the President to make it clear to Members and the Sejm that the criticisms of the Supreme Audit Chamber, which, for many years I remember, were not justified only because they ended with the sentiment that ends with the resolution of the committee. The Commission for State Control actually recommends not to adopt a report on the activities of the Supreme Audit Chamber in 2015. As far as I know, it's the first time a committee has ever given such a recommendation, treating it as a kind of disciplinary measure. However, it may be added that, at least in the last 5 years, which we have recently remembered, Members of the Supreme Audit Chamber have always submitted less or more critical comments to the Chamber. The terms of the proposal for a disciplinary measure in the form of failure to adopt the report relate to an assessment of the management of the Supreme Audit Chamber, partly concerning control activities, but above all concerning institutional problems. And now, in a few points, let me specify the shortcomings indicated at the committee's meeting. Control topics are by nature profiled and must be profiled. However, the inevitable result is that most of them refer to a narrow segment of our community life in general. Thus, the checks of NIK do not give a complete answer to the question of the size of the implementation or failure to carry out the so-called. horizontal risks identified in the country's development strategy. For example, I will mention one of them, namely a risk called low quality public services. At least 15 topics listed in Annex 2 to the report relate to this issue, and these are only topics coordinated by the departments of the Supreme Audit Chamber, i.e. not by field units, but there are no synthesiss and therefore no global systemic proposals for this risk. It is similar in other cases - the absence of super-professional system hazard synthesiss, because NIK has not developed methods for associating the results of various control topics over the years. Is such association possible? The first proposal in this area was made less than 20 years ago. Despite the passage of that time, the House, as I said, has not worked out a way in this regard. Should we wait another dozen years? The main expectation is that the House should submit proposals for global system solutions. It raises doubts, as we specifically discussed in the committee's meeting, the visibility in the NIK reports, including in the 2015 report, of the control of corruption issues. However, these controls have not added value for years, they have duplicated the arrangements for corruption mechanisms known from thousands of analyses carried out worldwide over the last few decades. The management of NIK consistently accepts the appearance of NIK discovery in this area. However, NIK controls only contain further illustrations - under Polish conditions - of various corruption mechanisms. Of course, this has some value, so there is no reason to stop registering other views of known phenomena. However, there is no reason to create appearances of extraordinaryness. Corruption should not be described as NIK does, but should be eradicated, and this is served by specialised institutions, in particular. Central Corruption Bureau. There is a particular reason for this criticism. Well, many years ago, when the Central Anti-corruption Office had not yet existed, and I don't think there was even a plan to set up such an institution, when the problems of corruption were in the shadows, especially in view of the media space, then NIK undertook a mission to bring this issue to light. At that time these controls were specifically programmed to unveil Polish manifestations of corrupt mechanisms and they were of great social and civic importance. NIK was then the first state institution to put corruption at the heart of public interest on such a scale. In this respect, years ago, the Supreme Audit Chamber played a role, you can say, missionary. But I did. Now that we have specialized institutions for this, when the area is perfectly described, it would be great if the current NIK leadership would give up to enriching with further illustrations of what we know not worst, and to combat what we have a relatively effective instrumentarium for, and instead take on another desirable civic mission. Can you find such an area? You can, at least, convince a lot of people. Let the House undertake the effort to reveal the signs of unacceptable level of injustice in Polish courts. In the past, NIK has effectively made the issue of corruption a front issue. Today let him undertake a mission to make the front issue of the destructive function of justice, the source of which is the Polish justice system. Is this a workable task? On an individual basis, in 2015, the judiciary accounted for 8% of complaints and requests from citizens addressed to NIK, while counting 6.5% of complaints and requests. It is therefore not small to say a comprehensive analytical material, which would probably increase in the light of the House's interest in the regularity and timeliness of judicial proceedings - that is, a quote from your report - and the validity of judicial decisions - again a quote from your report. Through extensive analysis of complaints coming to NIK, it would be possible to present a real picture of forensic pathology in Poland. Just in case, I'll remind you that discussing and commenting on court judgments is under no circumstances a violation of judicial independence. It is also time to question the way in which the financial results of the audit are determined. These effects have been defined for years by mixing real results with virtual ones, i.e. those that will occur in the future, perhaps they will occur or simply will not occur, which, of course, is difficult to predict at the starting point. Such a way of determining the effects makes it difficult to assess the real effectiveness and efficiency of the Supreme Audit Chamber. Such a modern way of determining the financial effects of the audit was adopted many years ago, but this does not mean that this condition is to be maintained until the end of the world or longer. Speaking of efficiency, you have to ask yourself, how about this? efficiency is influenced by extensive internal bureaucracy. In his speech, the President addressed this issue without presenting a real picture. Contrary to its name so-called. the quality assurance system of the control process does not perform an information or incentive function, nor does it affect the gratification of the controllers. First of all, I encourage all sceptics of modern bureaucratic art to read the 2015 resolution of President NIK on the quality assurance system of the control process, and more specifically the annexes to it. They can be used, among others. learn that currently, not controllers and their collaborators, advisors and directors are employees of quality, but, among other things, they are the guardians of quality of work. management support teams, control management groups and content teams, i.e. artificially established structures. Perhaps in order to increase the volume, or seriousness of the order, we can learn from it a lot of obviousness, namely that the tools for quality assurance are supervision, coordination, consultation, meetings. There are also creative novelties, namely various questionnaires, followed by registers and collections of completed questionnaires. I am unable to give a brief presentation of the wealth of reflections contained in this more than 70-page document, and I am only counting the annexes here. A lot of the statements there, if not most, are just obvious. A minor example is the directive that the controller should be the person who has the skills and knowledge required and the manager of the control team of the person who has the ability to organise the work of the team. These pseudoscience deliriums are a consequence of NIK's abandoning simple and effective management methods. Of course, these pseudo-scientific deliriums would have no meaning if they had not translated into the institutional practice already mentioned in the questionnaires. NIK employees must fill in these questionnaires, and undoubtedly do so, diplomatically speaking, routinely, maybe with a few exceptions. They know very well that the findings and assessments contained there do not affect the professional position of controllers and their gratifications. Building up the work of practitioners' controllers with dumber bureaucracy paves the way for a discrepancy between the reality of the paper bureaucracy and the reality of the day-to-day work of controllers. For example: a sloppy, disrespectful controller who overlooks valuable findings, but will respond to all items in the so-called. a checklist, has a better chance of getting a positive assessment than a responsible and gifted controller who will pick out any valuable finding, but will not answer any question from the checklist, because it will not have enough time imposed in advance. The construction of the work of practitioners' controllers is perhaps the most expressive expression of the silent disease, which is invisibly the special institution of our country, the Supreme Audit Chamber. Expansive internal bureaucracy restrains the creativity of controllers from birth. The idea of a quality assurance system for the control process introduced in March 2015, but of course it was also earlier, is missed. This is borne out by the fact that, after a year of management in this case, some provisions of the scheme have been amended by general letter, and after the next 5 months, already in August 2016, this order has simply been amended. The synthetic indicator indicating the number of controls per control worker shows a serious organisational weakness of NIK. In 2015, the Council NIK checked 2501 units with 1194 controllers, approximately 2500 and 1200, thus the indicator amounted to approximately 2 per capita unit controls, exactly 2.09 . There are, of course, no standards in this respect, but you can refer to data from years ago, when this ratio was not less than 3 and it was assumed that it should be around 4. It can therefore be concluded that the operational efficiency of NIK is unsatisfactory. As is known from public reports, the rules of employment at NIK are not transparent. Administrative workers were first employed without a public announcement, and later such announcements appeared, but then they resigned from this open form. Thus, under media pressure, the President of NIK issued in March 2015 Order No 17 on the introduction of the rules of recruitment into the NIK for posts of administrative staff and staff. § 2 of that provision provided for recruitment to be carried out through open and competitive selection procedures. The recruitment notice shall be published in the Chamber's Public Information Bulletin. Six months later, in September, the President issued Order No 48 on the opinion of candidates for employment in those posts which had repealed the earlier order. It was returned to the old practice, according to which it was enough to write a job application, of course, as long as you knew there was a vacancy in NIK. I wonder how many people have been employed at NIK since the end of September 2015 according to this discretionary practice? Press reports say more than 20. The taste of the case gives the fact that this did not bother the House in the context of the audit of public authorities to postulate, I quote: to take action to make public information about vacant places, rules and admission criteria public. Such duality reduces the prestige of the House, not to say: it discredits it, at least in the eyes of reasonable people. In order to be clear, I will extend the quote from the report on the control of traineeships and traineeships organised by the authorities. Here's a quote: The recruitment process for traineeships has often not been carried out in a transparent manner. Less than 1/5 of the audited offices have made public information on vacant places and recruitment rules, and the criteria for admission of candidates have not been laid down in a clear and written manner. Putting these two things together, the story of the splinter in the eye of the neighbor and the beam in his own eye is reminiscent. As you know, NIK has been manipulated by competitions for directors' positions, which so far results in the prosecution's request to take immunity from President NIK in connection with criminal charges. By combining these two issues, it is clear that the House does not care about reliable rules of employment in such a particular state institution. Since life can be paradoxical, in the margins of this case it may be worth noting that NIK is directing the work of an international team to update the code of ethics to be applied in the highest state control bodies concentrated in an international organisation. In December this year. The NIK will present a proposal at the Abu Dhabi Congress. Whether this code provides for acceptance of manipulative methods for hiring, we will see. The foundation of the good quality of the House's work is the audit staff, from which we should expect substantive preparation for work, integrity, integrity and availability in relation to the needs of our state community. Manipulating the rules of employment is not conducive to developing a culture of responsibility. There is yet another issue that has not yet been addressed with the issue of sound institutional order. Working at NIK by nature is a team-based thing, which everyone understands. Creating efficient teams to work on further audit topics ending in reports certainly does not encourage over-diversification of the status of employees. However, a serious problem is the serious difference between the institutional status of managers and the status of practitioners. Although the latter have many privileges, the highest salaries, the highest prizes, because prizes depend not on results, but on taking up a place in the hierarchy of employees, rather the stationary mode of work as opposed to practicing controllers, great freedom in the organisation of working time, as opposed to practicing controllers. In addition, it is not uncommon for directors to provide the necessary support to practitioners. Such a difference in professional status makes it difficult to develop a culture of cooperation aimed at achieving the objectives. The described malfunctions of the functioning of NIK are not some temporary excess that has occurred for a year or two years. In the last five years, as I mentioned at the beginning, and probably earlier, all the mentioned deficiencies, except for one, have been mentioned to the House on various occasions, including during the analysis of the NIK activity reports. Whether we like it or not, sooner or later the Sejm Commission for State Control had to face the prospect of disciplined the institution to which it has supervisory responsibilities. NIK had the opportunity to correct the organisation of the audit work, but it is not evident that it has benefited effectively, and therefore the majority proposal of the Sejm Commission for State Control is justified. In order to be clear, it is necessary to make clear at the end that the shortcomings identified concern the sphere of management of the Chamber, not the sphere of control crafts. Controllers, who mostly work professionally and sacrificially, and thanks to the work that we have valuable documentation, do not have adequate support from the heads of the Chamber, therefore the potential of this institution is not fully exploited. Thank you. [[Applause]]

It was my determination to record trials in courtrooms that led to my determination, contrary to the judicial environment.
