ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u489	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Yesterday, i.e. On September 12, we celebrated the 333rd anniversary of the Battle of Vienna. It was Jan III Sobieski who defeated the Ottoman Empire and protected Europe from Islamism. The Polish king commanded allies: Polish and German, which defeated the Turkish army besieged Vienna, thus inflicting one of the most severe disasters of the Ottoman Empire, which never rose from it again. This was a crucial moment for Europe. Following the defeat of Vienna, Turkey went to defense, and its centuries-old struggle for the transfer of Islam into the European continent was halted. Before the battle, King John III Sobieski, along with the Polish army, was at the Holy Mass at Kahlenberg, serving the Holy Mass and receiving the Holy Communion, and on the banners of the knights he ordered to write Mary's name. With such a cry, the Polish army went to battle with the Turks. After the victorious battle, the king wrote to Pope Innocent XI: venimus, vidimus et Deus vicit, i.e. We came, we saw, and God conquered. Pope Innocent, grateful to God for the victory of Vienna for the cause of Our Lady Mary, announced on 12 September the feast of Mary's name throughout the Church to commemorate the victorious battle of Christians over the followers of Islam. This year marks the 320th anniversary of the death of the great King John III Sobieski, who died on 17 June 1696 in the palace in Wilanów, Warsaw, and rests on Wawel in the crypt of St. Leonard. The Turkish Slayer left a will on three main issues, still up-to-date: reforming the state, stopping the opposition conspiring with foreign powers and defending Islam. One can say that this is the will of a great king for contemporary Poland. I would like to remind the universe of King John III Sobieski, who addressed to the voivodship sejmists: "Every Pole will warm up and attract to fight a son's love for his mother, his homeland, but also the glory of God, the desire to defend his own churches, the desire to preserve the freedom that our ancestors have fought, but also mercy for those Christian brothers who die in the war with Islam, respect for the shed blood of innocent civilians slaughtered under the occupation of the enemy. 300 years after these appeals, the arguments of the great king remain in force. To conclude, I would like to quote the important words of Professor Andrzej Nowak, which he gave in January 2013 at the Tuesday club meeting in Krakow: Poles have a mission to unify the peoples of Central and Eastern Europe, as well as to complete the work of evangelization of lay Europe, for which St. John Paul II. ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u493	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On August 28, we celebrated the 70th anniversary of the death of the paramedic and liaison 5. The Vilnius Brigade of the Home Army of Danuta Siedzikovny ps. Inka. I would therefore like to say a few words about the life of this heroine from the division of Major Slimpy. Danuta Sidzikówna was born on 3 September 1928. She was the daughter of Wacław Sidziek, a forester, and Eugene from the house of Tyminska. She had two sisters, Wiesława and Irene. During the war, Danuta Siedzikovna studied at the Salesian Sisters' School in Rosarystok. In December 1943, she joined the Home Army, where she received medical training. Less than a year later, Inka started working in Hajnówka's fortress. When she was 17, she was arrested for collaborating with an anti-communist underground. She was released by the Vilnius patrol of AK Stanisław Wołoncej Konusa, then as a paramedic took up duty in the Konusa branch and then the squadron por. Jana Mazura, Piasta and por. Mariana Pucińskiego.Mścisława. For a short time her superior was also Lor. Leon Beynar, Deputy Scoundrel. That's when Danuta Sidzikówna assumed Inka's alias. At the turn of 1945 and 1946, as Danuta Obuchowicz, she began working in Miłomłyn's fortress. She made contact with the priest at the time. Zdzisław Badocha, the commander of one of the squadrons of Skull. After his death, she was sent to Gdansk for medical supplies. There on July 20, 1946, in an apartment on the street. Wróblewski 7 in Wrzeszcz, one of the contact locations 5. The Vilnius AK Brigade, whose addresses were revealed previously by the security detail Regina Żylińska-Mordas, was arrested. She was placed in pavilion V of the prison in Gdansk. She was beaten and humiliated, and yet she didn't give any testimony that would incriminate the members of the Vilnius AK brigades. Ince, among others, was accused. involved in attacks on MO and UB officers. However, there were discrepancies in the statements of the militia themselves. One of them admitted that she gave him first aid when he was injured. Danuta Sidzikówna was sentenced to death on August 3, 1946, at the age of 17. The defender, Mr Inka, asked President Bolesław Bierut for mercy. Unfortunately, it didn't work. In the flu to the Nicholas' sisters from Gdansk shortly before she died, she wrote: "Tell my grandmother that I behaved properly." The sentence was executed on 28 August 1946. Danuta Sidzikówna was shot with Felix Selmanowicz ps. Zagończyn by the commander of the firing squad. Franciszek Sawicki in prison at ul. Chicken in Gdansk. During the period of the communist propaganda communist propaganda called "Inka" a bandit. In 1991, on the other hand, the Provincial Court in Gdańsk found the judgment of the Military District Court condemning Danuta Sidzikówna as invalid. The burial site of Danuta Sidzikówny until 2014 was unknown. On 8 January 2015 Krzysztof Szwagrzyk, the leader of a group of scientists, provided information on the location of the remains of Danuta Sidzikówny. On 28 August 2016, the state funeral of Danuta Sidzikówna and Felix Selmanowicz ps. Zagończyk at the Garrison Cemetery in Gdansk. The President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda issued a decision to appoint Danuta Sidzikówna as first rank officer. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-09-13-sejm-25-1.u503	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Last week was the 77th anniversary of the Battle of Bzura. This historic event is referred to as the biggest clash of the September campaign and the biggest battle in the lap in the early stages of the war. On September 9, 1939, the army of Poznań and the army of Pomerania began to attack. The first stage of the battle was the attack on Stryków, where our soldiers surprised a disoriented enemy and achieved considerable success. Then the white-red troops began attacking the occupied enemy of Łowicz. This was a time when our army had achieved considerable success and drove the enemy into serious problems, which forced the Germans to bring additional troops to the front. The final stage of the battle was the retreat towards Warsaw. It was only when the force-strength enemy proved to be too numerous to be able to fight them. The Polish Army showed extraordinary heroism and fought a heroic fight for freedom with many more troops of the opponent. This battle was of great importance, and it was permanently recorded on history cards. Resistance and fierceness in the fight of our soldiers delayed the attack of the enemy and provided such a valuable time to prepare the defense of Warsaw. I am pleased that the local authorities have been very involved in commemorating these historical events. The municipalities, in which 77 years ago there were fights, celebrated the memory of the fallen, organizing ceremonies with the participation of the Polish Army. The 1939 events testify to the heroism of our soldiers. They were commemorated in such places. villages, such as Łęczyca or Friday. These are places where once our compatriots in eagle uniforms gave their lives in the name of the highest values. The celebrations of the anniversary were not only symbolic. We honored all those who took part in the battle. We must remember those who have shown true courage and an attitude worthy of imitation. Today we consider them national heroes, who did not look at the number of their opponents outnumbered and the odds they had in this fight. They stood together in defense of their homeland, and this was their goal during difficult fights. It is this huge charisma, the courage of our army that we can live in a free country today, use Polish language and celebrate celebrations under the white and red flag. The Polish Army was appreciated by many nations for their efforts during World War II. However, those who should remember them always and everywhere are us, Poles. Remembering their independence and fierceness in the fight of our soldiers, each of us should treat them proudly and respectfully. It was because of events such as the Battle of Bzura that the Polish army became a role model for the nation. I would once again like to express my joy that the fights of our soldiers are commemorated, and history is passed on to young generations. Thank you. [[Applause]]

On stage you can listen to concerts of bands playing different types of music, from jazz to folk to klezmer music. It is worth mentioning that Radom's history is very rich, and our city has always participated in the most important historical events. Therefore, there are always concerts of courtyard bands playing so well-known Polish folk music, and there is always a place for the klezmer band, which reminds residents of the non-existent but so numerous community of Radom Jews. In addition to the concerts took place, as every year, the Rally of the Old Automobiles - the prize is the Mayor's Cup of Radom - where we could admire many beautiful cars from the past era. In the outdoors, Radom Military Orchestra presented her skills this year, and we were also honored to present prizes for the winners 48. Polish Airplane Acrobat Championship. The street of Tradition is a multifaceted event. Apart from the events on stage, among others. in Resurs, or in the streets of the Old Town, the Radomians could see many lectures with comments from outstanding historians about interwar aviation or the history of Radom from the first days of occupation. Every year the Street of Tradition is organized under a different slogan, this year under the slogan "Aviations and Variations," therefore so many events were devoted to aviation, especially to this interwar one. There have been many lectures and demonstrations of old aircrafts, there have also been memories of heroic airmen from our surroundings, but it was not only the theory that encouraged Radomians to learn about this subject, but also many practical events such as urban play, flight simulator or gyroscope exercises. Ladies and gentlemen, there have also been many performances, including: musical Hallo Szpicbródka and a performance of the town of R., based on the works of Radom writers, as well as seances and lectures concerning old films. For ladies there was a fashion show inspired by costumes from the era prepared by the School of Leather and Clothes Styling and Services Team and a competition for the best outfit among the audience, and also concerts and tango shows, which were tango in such a favourable atmosphere could be danced. In the afternoon, there was a concert "Singing Everyone Can" which was performed by Radom artists, presenting former hits.
